Here's my complete analysis of the codebase and what adding new data sources entails:

## Current Architecture Summary

The system is a **UK public procurement intelligence platform** with 3 implemented layers (L1-L3) and a working ingestion pipeline. Here's the flow:

### Existing Sources
Currently **4 sources** are registered in `crates/daemon/src/sources/registry.rs:562`:
- **`fts`** (Find a Tender) — procurement, daily 4:15 AM cron, has canary health checks
- **`contracts-finder`** — procurement, daily 4:45 AM cron, no canary
- **`alibaba`** / **`1688`** — supplier sources (for marketplace enrichment, not procurement ingestion)

### How a Source Works End-to-End

Each procurement source follows this path:

1. **Source registry** (`sources/registry.rs`) — declares the source with its `SourceDefinition`: kind, skill bindings (production/canary/auth-refresh), cron expressions, circuit breaker policy

2. **CLI entry point** (`main.rs:986`) — `--ingest {source_id}` dispatches to a source-specific `run_{source}_live_loop()` function

3. **Live loop function** (e.g., `main.rs:4321` for contracts-finder):
   - Loads the skill from `skills/procurement-ingestion/{source_id}/`
   - Reads cursor from `source_cursors` table
   - Creates `workflow_jobs` row (kind: `sync_source`)
   - Executes skill via `execute_skill_session()` (spawns Claude CLI in PTY)
   - Parses `result.json` from the agent session
   - Calls `persist_ingestion_from_agent_output()` to write to SQLite
   - Handles continuation (enqueue follow-up if `continuation_required=true`)

4. **Persistence** (`main.rs:5401`) — generic function that writes:
   - `raw_documents` — raw JSON/content
   - `procurement_processes` — canonical process records
   - `contract_notices` — notice details with upsert on `(source_id, source_notice_id)`
   - `notice_lots` — lot breakdowns
   - `source_cursors` — updated cursor state

5. **Skill package** (e.g., `skills/procurement-ingestion/contracts-finder/`) — contains:
   - `SKILL.md` — YAML frontmatter (metadata) + markdown playbook (agent instructions)
   - `result.schema.json` — JSON Schema for result contract
   - `checkpoint.schema.json` — checkpoint format for resumption
   - `canary.inputs.jsonl` — canary test inputs
   - `golden.assertions.yaml` — assertion rules for validation

### Key Patterns for New Sources

The two new sources differ fundamentally from the existing ones:

| Aspect | Contracts Finder / FTS | NHS Supply Chain (Jaggaer) | ProContract (Due North) |
|--------|----------------------|---------------------------|------------------------|
| **Access** | Public API (OCDS) | Authenticated portal | Authenticated portal |
| **Data format** | JSON API responses | HTML portal + PDF downloads | HTML portal + PDF downloads |
| **Auth** | None | Username/password login | Username/password login |
| **Document types** | Links in API metadata | PDF tender packs behind login | PDF tender packs behind login |
| **Interaction** | `agent-browser` → API endpoint | `agent-browser` → login → navigate portal → scrape + download | `agent-browser` → login → navigate → scrape + download |

### What Adding a New Source Requires

**Per source, you need:**

1. **Skill package** — `skills/procurement-ingestion/{source_id}/`
   - `SKILL.md` with playbook for the agent to navigate the portal, login, scrape notices, download PDFs
   - `result.schema.json` — same top-level shape (`cursor`, `continuation_required`, `notices[]`) but notice fields adapted to what's available on that portal
   - `checkpoint.schema.json`, `canary.inputs.jsonl`, `golden.assertions.yaml`

2. **Auth refresh skill** — `skills/auth-refresh/{source_id}/`
   - For handling session expiry, cookie refresh, re-login
   - Declared as `auth_refresh_binding` on the source definition

3. **Source health skill** (optional) — `skills/source-health/{source_id}/`
   - Lightweight canary that checks the portal is up and login works
   - Declared as `canary_binding`

4. **Source definition** — new entry in `builtin_definitions()` in `registry.rs`
   - Production binding pointing to `procurement-ingestion/{source_id}`
   - Auth refresh binding pointing to `auth-refresh/{source_id}`
   - Optional canary binding
   - Config JSON (may include portal URL, credentials reference)

5. **Live loop function** — new `run_{source}_live_loop()` in `main.rs` (or generalize the existing pattern)
   - Match arm in the `--ingest` handler at `main.rs:986`

6. **`sources` table insert** — row in SQLite for the new source

7. **PDF integration** — these portals serve tender documents as PDFs, so the new ingestion skills need to:
   - Download PDFs from the portal (save to `raw_documents` with `storage_path`)
   - Create `tender_document_sets` linking PDFs to the process
   - The existing PDF extraction pipeline (Docling structural → semantic overlay skills) then handles the rest automatically via `extract_document_structure` and `extract_document_semantics` job kinds

### The PDF Extraction Toolkit Connection

This is critical for the new sources. Unlike Contracts Finder (which exposes data via OCDS JSON API), NHS Supply Chain and ProContract likely keep most contract detail inside PDF tender packs. The recently-built PDF pipeline is exactly what's needed:

- **Structural extraction** (`pdf/engine.rs`) — Docling parses the PDF into markdown/tables/sections
- **Policy routing** (`pdf/policy.rs`) — classifies document role and picks appropriate semantic skill
- **Semantic extraction** — 4 overlay skills extract domain data:
  - `pdf-extraction/tender-specification` → lot structure, requirements, standards
  - `pdf-extraction/pricing-schedule` → line items, quantities, rates
  - `pdf-extraction/bill-of-quantities` → itemized quantities
  - `pdf-extraction/base` → fallback generic extraction
- **Database** — results stored in `document_extractions` + `document_semantic_extractions`

The ingestion skill for each new source would download PDFs into `raw_documents`, and then downstream jobs would trigger the PDF pipeline automatically.

### Credential Handling

The new sources require login. The existing pattern (from alibaba/1688 auth skills) shows:
- Credentials would be stored in `.env` or `sources.config` JSON (not in skill playbooks)
- The auth-refresh skill handles browser-based login flows
- Auth state is maintained across sessions via the browser profile directory (`CLAUDE_BROWSER_PROFILE_DIR`)
- The circuit breaker pattern handles auth failures gracefully (challenge threshold → circuit open → cooldown)

The persist function `persist_ingestion_from_agent_output` is already **source-agnostic** — it just needs `source_id` and a result JSON matching the notices schema. So the heavy lifting for each new source is primarily in the **skill playbooks** (teaching the agent how to navigate and extract from each specific portal).

# Intent-to-Handoff — Intelligent Handoff Prompt Generator

## Philosophy

This skill transforms raw user intent into a self-contained handoff prompt that a fresh Claude Code session (with ZERO prior context) can execute autonomously.

```
┌─────────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────────────┐
│   USER INTENT   │ ──► │  INTELLIGENT RESEARCH │ ──► │   HANDOFF PROMPT (.md)  │
│                 │     │                       │     │                         │
│ "I want to add  │     │ • Discover project    │     │ • MISSION statement     │
│  feature X to   │     │ • Explore codebase    │     │ • File-specific reading │
│  this project"  │     │ • Map architecture    │     │ • Embedded types/APIs   │
│                 │     │ • Scan docs/specs     │     │ • Constraints & rules   │
│                 │     │ • Find conventions    │     │ • Concrete deliverables │
└─────────────────┘     └──────────────────────┘     └─────────────────────────┘
```

The user describes WHAT they want done. The skill figures out HOW to research it, WHAT context is needed, and synthesizes a document that stands completely on its own.

**Key insight**: A good handoff prompt doesn't just list file paths — it embeds the actual type definitions, API signatures, and architectural patterns that the receiving session needs. "Read types.ts" is insufficient. The actual type shapes must be right there in the prompt.

## When to Use

Trigger phrases:
- "Create a handoff prompt for..."
- "Prepare a prompt for a new session to..."
- "Generate a handoff for [feature/task]"
- "Write a prompt to hand off [task]"
- "Create a session prompt for..."
- "Handoff [feature/task]"
- "I need a fresh session to [do X], prepare the context"
- "/intent-to-handoff" or "/handoff"

## The 6-Phase Workflow

```
Phase 0          Phase 1           Phase 2              Phase 3
PROJECT     ──►  INTENT       ──►  CODEBASE        ──►  DOC & CONSTRAINT
DISCOVERY        CAPTURE           EXPLORATION          SCAN
(auto)           (interactive)     (subagents)          (auto)
                                        │
                                        ▼
Phase 5          Phase 4           ┌─────────┐
SELF-        ◄── PROMPT       ◄──  │ Context │
VERIFICATION     SYNTHESIS         │ Assembly│
(auto)           (auto)            └─────────┘
    │
    ▼
 OUTPUT: ./prompts/{slug}-handoff.md
```

**Phase 0 — Project Discovery**: Detect project type, languages, framework, docs, and structure. Fully automatic.

**Phase 1 — Intent Capture**: Parse the user's request. Ask 1-3 clarifying questions if needed (scope, audience, known files). Build an intent model.

**Phase 2 — Codebase Exploration**: Launch 2-3 Explore subagents to search for relevant files, map architecture around the affected area, and find related docs/tests.

**Phase 3 — Documentation & Constraint Scan**: Read project README/CLAUDE.md, relevant specs, and discover coding conventions, architectural patterns, and constraints.

**Phase 4 — Prompt Synthesis**: Select the appropriate template (Feature/Bug/Refactor), assemble all sections, embed key type definitions and API signatures directly into the prompt.

**Phase 5 — Self-Verification**: Run the quality checklist. Verify all file paths exist, ensure self-containment, fix issues, and report to the user.

See **METHODOLOGY.md** for detailed execution instructions per phase.

## Quick Reference

| Item | Convention |
|------|-----------|
| **Output location** | `./prompts/{feature-slug}-handoff.md` (overridable) |
| **Naming** | Kebab-case slug derived from the task, e.g. `add-auth-flow-handoff.md` |
| **Length target** | 200-500 lines typical, up to 800 for complex multi-feature tasks |
| **Templates** | Feature (A), Bug (B), Refactor/Migration (C) — see TEMPLATES.md |
| **Quality gates** | CHECKLIST.md — run before every output |

## Supporting Files

- **METHODOLOGY.md** — Detailed phase-by-phase execution guide with tool usage, gate criteria, and decision trees
- **TEMPLATES.md** — Output prompt templates for Feature, Bug, and Refactor/Migration task types
- **CHECKLIST.md** — Quality verification checklist for self-verification (Phase 5)

## Execution Notes

- **Subagent usage**: Phase 2 uses Explore subagents for parallel codebase research. Launch them with specific, targeted queries based on the intent model.
- **Embedded context**: Always prefer embedding key types/signatures over just referencing file paths. A fresh session should not need to do redundant exploration.
- **Self-containment test**: After writing the prompt, mentally simulate: "If I opened a brand new session and pasted ONLY this document, could I execute the task?" If no, the prompt is incomplete.
- **ULTRATHINK**: Every output prompt MUST include the "ULTRATHINK and be meticulous" directive.
- **No session leakage**: Never reference "our discussion", "as we discussed", or any context from the current session in the output prompt. It must stand alone.


ARGUMENTS: Build, agentically test, and integrate the NHS Supply Chain (Jaggaer) procurement ingestion data source.

## Source Details
- Platform: NHS Supply Chain via Jaggaer e-Sourcing
- URL: nhssupplychain.app.jaggaer.com/esop/ogc-host/private/nhs_supply_chain/second_s.jst?_ncp=1776085033026.79411-1
- Login: zacfarrell@icloud.com / Z4c1sG4y@2026
- Source ID: nhs-supply-chain
- Source Kind: Procurement
- Credentials are safe to embed directly in the SKILL.md playbook (no auth-refresh skill needed)

## What Must Be Built

### Phase 1: Skill Build + Agentic Validation Loop

1. **Deep codebase study** — The agent MUST read and understand:
   - The existing contracts-finder skill package: `skills/procurement-ingestion/contracts-finder/` (SKILL.md, result.schema.json, checkpoint.schema.json, canary.inputs.jsonl, golden.assertions.yaml)
   - The existing FTS skill package: `skills/procurement-ingestion/fts/`
   - The persistence function `persist_ingestion_from_agent_output` in `crates/daemon/src/main.rs` (~line 5401) — this is source-agnostic and defines exactly what fields the result contract must include
   - The database schema in `migrations/0001_sqlite_state_initial.sql`, `0003_sqlite_runtime.sql`, `0006_pdf_extraction_toolkit.sql` — understand every table the ingestion touches (raw_documents, procurement_processes, contract_notices, notice_lots, tender_document_sets, source_cursors)
   - The PDF extraction toolkit: `crates/daemon/src/pdf/engine.rs`, `policy.rs`, `chunking.rs` — understand how PDFs flow through structural→semantic extraction
   - The PDF extraction skills: `skills/pdf-extraction/*/SKILL.md` — understand what overlay skills exist
   - The source registry: `crates/daemon/src/sources/registry.rs` — understand SourceDefinition structure and builtin_definitions()
   - The contracts-finder live loop function in main.rs (~line 4321) — understand the full execution flow from skill load → session → persist → continuation
   - The auth-refresh skills: `skills/auth-refresh/alibaba/SKILL.md` — as reference for authentication patterns in PTY interactive skills
   - The error manifest system: `crates/daemon/src/errors/manifest.rs` and `markdown.rs`

2. **Navigate the portal manually first** — Before writing any skill, the agent MUST use agent-browser to:
   - Open the Jaggaer portal URL
   - Log in with the provided credentials
   - Explore the dashboard/UI to understand the portal structure
   - Find where opportunities/tenders/contracts are listed
   - Understand pagination, filtering, and search mechanisms
   - Identify what data fields are available on listing pages vs detail pages
   - Identify where PDF tender documents are attached/downloadable
   - Map the portal's data model to our result contract schema
   - Document what's directly available vs what requires PDF extraction
   - Take screenshots/notes of the portal structure for the skill playbook

3. **Build the skill package** — Create `skills/procurement-ingestion/nhs-supply-chain/`:
   - `SKILL.md` — Full playbook with:
     - YAML frontmatter (skill_id, interaction_profile: pty_interactive, model_profile: browse_stable_low_cost, agent_cli: claude, etc.)
     - Login credentials embedded in the playbook
     - Step-by-step instructions for: login → navigate to opportunities → paginate → extract notice data → download PDF documents → build result contract
     - Field mapping from portal data to result contract schema
     - PDF document download instructions (save to raw_documents with storage_path)
     - Cursor/continuation semantics for incremental ingestion
     - Failure codes and resume strategy
     - Run manifest instructions (copy from contracts-finder)
   - `result.schema.json` — Must match the same top-level shape as contracts-finder (cursor, continuation_required, notices[]) so persist_ingestion_from_agent_output works. Notice fields adapted to what this portal provides.
   - `checkpoint.schema.json` — Checkpoint format for safe resumption
   - `canary.inputs.jsonl` — Canary test input
   - `golden.assertions.yaml` — Assertion rules

4. **Agentic validation loop** — After building the skill, the agent MUST test it:
   - Invoke a sub-agent to actually execute the skill (run it via Claude CLI in the same way the daemon would)
   - The sub-agent should: open the portal, login, navigate, extract data, write result.json
   - Verify the result.json validates against result.schema.json
   - Verify the data can be persisted via persist_ingestion_from_agent_output (check field completeness)
   - If ANY errors occur: use the error-manifest skill to create an error manifest, then fix the skill and re-test
   - Loop until the skill is robust: login works, data extraction works, result contract is valid, PDF documents are identified
   - Only proceed to Phase 2 after validation passes

### Phase 2: Daemon Integration + Automated Runtime Test

1. **Source registry entry** — Add nhs-supply-chain to builtin_definitions() in registry.rs:
   - SourceKind::Procurement
   - Production binding: procurement-ingestion/nhs-supply-chain
   - No canary binding initially
   - No auth_refresh_binding (credentials in skill)
   - SourcePolicy::default()
   - Appropriate cron expression

2. **Live loop function** — Add run_nhs_supply_chain_live_loop() in main.rs:
   - Follow the exact same pattern as run_contracts_finder_live_loop (line 4321)
   - Load skill, read cursor, build job params, create job, claim, execute PTY session, parse result, persist, handle continuation
   - Add match arm in the --ingest handler (line 986): "nhs-supply-chain" => ...

3. **Sources table insert** — Ensure the source row exists in SQLite

4. **Integration test** — Write an integration test similar to agent_contracts_finder_ingestion.rs that validates:
   - Skill directory structure is complete
   - Frontmatter metadata is correct
   - Result schema is valid
   - Canary assertions are meaningful
   - Live loop can execute end-to-end

5. **E2E validation** — Run the actual ingestion via `--ingest nhs-supply-chain` and verify:
   - Notices appear in contract_notices table
   - Processes created in procurement_processes
   - Lots extracted to notice_lots
   - PDF documents referenced (if any)
   - Cursor updated in source_cursors
   - Data visible in Tauri desktop app

## Critical Constraints
- The result contract MUST be compatible with persist_ingestion_from_agent_output — same top-level shape
- Notice IDs must be prefixed with source identifier (e.g., "nhs-" prefix)
- Process IDs derived from notice identifiers in a deterministic way
- All monetary values in pence (integer), currency field set
- Dates in ISO 8601
- The skill uses pty_interactive interaction_profile (browser automation required)
- PDF documents found on tenders should be tracked in the result for downstream pipeline processing
- The skill must handle: login flows, session timeouts, portal-specific pagination, rate limiting

## Two Handoff Prompts Created

### 1. `prompts/nhs-supply-chain-source-handoff.md` (26.8 KB)
**Target:** NHS Supply Chain via Jaggaer e-Sourcing portal
- Source ID: `nhs-supply-chain`
- Notice ID prefix: `nhs-`
- Cron: 5:30 AM daily (staggered after Contracts Finder at 4:45 AM)

### 2. `prompts/procontract-source-handoff.md` (29.2 KB)
**Target:** ProContract by Due North
- Source ID: `procontract`
- Notice ID prefix: `pc-`
- Cron: 6:00 AM daily (staggered after NHS at 5:30 AM)

### Structure of Each Handoff

Both follow the same two-phase pattern:

**Phase 1 — Skill Build + Agentic Validation Loop:**
1. **Deep codebase study** — mandatory reading list of 12-13 files (persist function, live loop, registry, schema, PDF pipeline, error manifests, reference skills)
2. **Portal exploration** — agent-browser discovery checklist: login flow, listing structure, pagination, data fields, PDF document locations, URL patterns
3. **Skill package build** — 5 files: SKILL.md (with credentials embedded), result.schema.json, checkpoint.schema.json, canary.inputs.jsonl, golden.assertions.yaml
4. **Agentic validation loop** — sub-agent executes the skill, validates result.json against schema and persistence requirements, uses error-manifest skill on failures, iterates until robust

**Phase 2 — Daemon Integration:**
1. Source registry entry in `registry.rs`
2. Sources table insert
3. Live loop function + `--ingest` match arm
4. Compile + clippy verification
5. E2E validation with SQLite queries + desktop app check

### Key Design Decisions Embedded

- **No auth-refresh skills** — credentials embedded directly in SKILL.md playbooks
- **`access_route: "portal_authenticated"`** — distinguishes from public sources
- **`persist_ingestion_from_agent_output` compatibility** — exact field requirements documented inline with required/optional annotations
- **PDF document awareness** — documents listed in result contract for downstream Docling + semantic extraction pipeline
- **Error manifest loop** — structured debugging cycle with ErrorCode taxonomy for each failure mode
- **ProContract-specific mapping guidance** — status vocabulary mapping, no OCID (use portal reference), value parsing from formatted strings to pence

## Updated Handoffs

### `prompts/nhs-supply-chain-source-handoff.md` (26 KB, 623 lines)
Unchanged from earlier -- single-skill ingestion for NHS Supply Chain / Jaggaer. Two-phase approach (skill build + daemon integration) with agentic validation loop.

### `prompts/procontract-source-handoff.md` (50 KB, 1127 lines) -- COMPLETELY REWRITTEN
Now describes a **three-stage procurement pipeline** with three independent skills:

| Skill | ID | Side Effects | Cron | Purpose |
|-------|------|-------------|------|---------|
| **Step 1: Discovery** | `procontract-discover` | `none` | 5:00 AM | Scrape Opportunities listing, visit each detail page, save high-level contract data to DB |
| **Step 2: Interest Registration** | `procontract-register-interest` | `external_mutation` | 5:30 AM | Pre-filter new contracts (goods not services, expiry > 1 month), navigate portal and click "Request Interest" on qualifying ones, track in `process_stage_status` |
| **Step 3: Tender Enrichment** | `procontract-enrich-tender` | `none` | 6:00 AM | Check Home > "Recently Added" for approved tenders, open Activity Dashboard, click the NEW message (not the interest expression), extract full details from RFx Response page, download ALL attachments (PDF/DOCX/ZIP), update DB with enriched data + deadline |

### Key Design Decisions in the Rewrite

- **Three separate source IDs** in the registry (`procontract`, `procontract-interest`, `procontract-enrich`) with staggered cron schedules
- **Step 1 reuses `persist_ingestion_from_agent_output`** -- no daemon changes needed for discovery
- **Steps 2 & 3 need custom persist functions** since their output shapes differ (`registrations[]` and `enriched_tenders[]`)
- **`process_stage_status`** tracks pipeline progression (`interest_registration` and `tender_enrichment` stages)
- **Activity Dashboard navigation** is explicitly documented with the critical instruction: open the NEW message, never click the Expression of Interest entry (matching the annotated screenshot)
- **DOCX support** documented -- Docling handles DOCX natively, plus ZIP extraction for appendices
- **Each skill has its own agentic validation loop** (Phase 1A/1B/1C) before any daemon integration (Phase 2)
- **All screenshots referenced** with field mappings, URL patterns, and expected modal content embedded in the handoff

### Assistant (2026-04-13T14:40:07)

I've re-read both handoffs with fresh eyes. I've identified several critical gaps. Let me fix them:

**Issues found:**

1. **Skills can't query the database.** Skills run as Claude CLI in a PTY — they have NO SQLite access. The daemon provides context via `job_parameters`. Steps 2 & 3 of ProContract fundamentally need the daemon to query DB and pass contract lists to the skill. The handoff glosses over this.

2. **The `persist_ingestion_from_agent_output` code isn't embedded.** I reference it by line number but the methodology says embed actual signatures. A fresh agent can't read "line 5401" without file access.

3. **The agentic validation loop is vague.** "Invoke a sub-agent" with no practical how-to. The agent needs to know: use `--skill-path` to test, or use the Agent tool with specific instructions.

4. **State paths are inconsistent.** Mix of `state/state.db` (local dev) and `$HOME/Library/Application Support/...` (Tauri). Should clarify both.

5. **Missing `--skill-path` testing mode.** The existing contracts-finder handoff shows testing via `--skill-path` before live loop integration. Neither handoff mentions this.

6. **NHS handoff doesn't acknowledge the portal is totally unknown.** No screenshots exist for Jaggaer — the exploration phase needs stronger guidance.

Let me apply targeted fixes to both files.

Here's what I fixed across both handoffs:

### NHS Supply Chain Handoff Improvements

1. **Added `--skill-path` testing mode** -- the existing way to test a skill before live loop integration, which the original handoff completely omitted
2. **Clarified state paths** -- explained local `state/` for dev vs `$HOME/Library/Application Support/...` for Tauri, preventing path confusion
3. **Embedded actual persistence function code** -- replaced the field list with the actual Rust extraction calls showing exactly what's required vs optional and what the defaults are
4. **Highlighted the `ocid` collision trap** -- if `ocid` is left empty, `process_id` becomes `"proc-"` for ALL notices, causing a catastrophic collision. Added bold warning.
5. **Strengthened portal exploration** -- added explicit "WARNING: No screenshots available" notice, detailed 6-step discovery protocol with screenshot instructions, and advice to save exploration notes to a file before writing the playbook
6. **Made validation loop practical** -- replaced vague "invoke a sub-agent" with actual Agent tool prompt template and the `--skill-path` daemon alternative. Added specific validation commands.
7. **Added AGENTS.md reference** -- the CLAUDE.md says it's the single source of truth
8. **Fixed E2E validation queries** -- added orphan check query, used local state paths

### ProContract Handoff Improvements

1. **Added the database access constraint section** -- explicitly documented that skills run in PTY with NO SQLite access, and the daemon must provide context via `job_parameters`. This was the most critical missing piece.
2. **Rewrote Step 2's architecture** -- the pre-filtering logic now correctly lives in the daemon live loop function, not the skill. The daemon queries DB, filters, passes `contracts_to_register[]` to the skill.
3. **Fixed Step 3's description** -- clarified this skill uniquely discovers work from the portal (not from DB) since the "Recently Added" page is the only way to know if buyers responded
4. **Embedded persistence function code** -- same as NHS, with the `ocid` collision warning
5. **Added `--skill-path` and state path guidance** -- same as NHS
6. **Made Skill 1 validation loop practical** -- concrete Agent tool prompt, `--skill-path` alternative, checklist format
7. **Fixed all E2E commands** -- proper `--state-root state` flags, added orphan check

### What is NOT done — the critical miss:

**The daemon cannot actually execute these skills end-to-end.** The very NEXT commit after the pipeline commit is `8119e18 Add ProContract runtime blocker manifest`, which documents **three unresolved blockers**:

1. **Silent-success bug** (`run_bound_skill()` at main.rs:3515) — when a procurement skill exits without `result.json`, the manual `--skill-path` path marks the job `completed` instead of `failed`. This masked the real failure.

2. **Codex adapter probe incompatibility** — `CodexAdapter::probe()` sends `codex probe --json` but the installed `codex-cli 0.120.0` doesn't support that flag. Unlike the Claude adapter (which has a fallback), Codex hard-fails.

3. **Nested Claude PTY API failure** — the spawned Claude CLI session hits an Anthropic API 500 error before producing `result.json`. The PTY capture shows: `API Error: 500 {"type":"error","error":{"type":"api_error","message":"Internal server error"}}`

The data in the database (100 notices, stage tracking, documents) came from **Phase 1 agentic validation** (sub-agents running the skills directly via agent-browser), not from successful daemon execution. The daemon-driven path has not completed successfully.

## What's Next in the QA Queue

The error manifest at `prompts/procontract-runtime-blocker-error-manifest.md` already has a detailed investigation roadmap. In priority order:

**1. Fix the silent-success bug (H1)** — Patch `run_bound_skill()` so missing `result.json` on procurement skills = failed job, not completed. This is the most important fix because it prevents false-positive success masking real failures.

**2. Fix the Codex adapter probe (H2)** — Add a fallback like the Claude adapter has, or update the probe command to match what `codex-cli 0.120.0` actually supports.

**3. Re-test with a working agent runtime (H3)** — After H1+H2, re-run `--skill-path` to see if the nested Claude API error was transient or persistent.

**4. Run the actual `--ingest` live loops** — Once `--skill-path` works, run all three stages sequentially via `--ingest procontract`, `--ingest procontract-interest`, `--ingest procontract-enrich` and verify data lands correctly through the daemon path.

### Key Observations

**The pipeline is stalled at document extraction.** This is the critical bottleneck:
- 37 of 46 structural extraction jobs **failed** (Docling)
- All 39 semantic extraction jobs **failed** (Claude overlay skills)
- 0 `document_semantic_extractions` exist
- 1 structural job is still stuck in `running` state

Until extraction works, you can't get rich lot/product data from PDFs — and everything downstream (product candidates, marketplace search, pricing) depends on it.

**Stages 3, 4a, 4b, 6 have working code but have never run on real data.** The deterministic pipeline (`--pipeline procontract`) implements these stages but they've never been executed against the 100 ingested contracts. The TypeScript intelligence libraries (`qualifyGoodsOpportunity`, `resolveProcessRelations`, `computeCoverageRollup`) are fully implemented but not wired into the daemon flow for real execution.

**Stages 7-12 are fixture-only.** The daemon can materialize fake data for these stages using `persist_pipeline_materialization()` for testing, but there's no real implementation. These stages represent the core commercial intelligence that needs building.

---

### The Linear Path Forward

```
PHASE A: UNBLOCK THE PIPELINE
──────────────────────────────
 A1. Fix runtime blockers (handoff created → another agent)
 A2. Fix document extraction failures
     - Diagnose why 37 structural extractions failed (Docling errors?)
     - Diagnose why 39 semantic extractions failed (skill errors?)
     - Get extraction completion rate to >80%
     → Result: semantic data flows into document_semantic_extractions

PHASE B: RUN EXISTING CODE ON REAL DATA
────────────────────────────────────────
 B1. Run deterministic pipeline on real contracts
     - Stage 3: Relate (OCID dedup across CF + FTS + ProContract)
     - Stage 4a: Prequalify Notice (CPV goods classification, expiry check)
     - Stage 6: Extract Lots (implicit lots + PDF-derived lots)
     - Stage 4b: Qualify Lot (per-lot goods/services filter)
     → Result: qualification_runs populated, goods vs services separated,
       expired contracts filtered, physical goods identified

 B2. Validate the filtering works
     - How many contracts survive prequalification?
     - Are the goods/services classifications accurate?
     - Does the 30-day expiry check filter correctly?

PHASE C: BUILD PRODUCT EXTRACTION
──────────────────────────────────
 C1. Stage 7: Extract Products (NEEDS BUILDING)
     - Agent-driven skill that reads tender documents + semantic extractions
     - Outputs: ProductCandidate per lot (name, headNoun, candidateType,
       matchArchetype, quantity, specifications, standards)
     - This is what turns "Supply of Mobile Plant" into structured product
       records with searchable attributes
     → Result: product_candidates table populated

 C2. Stage 8: Score Quoteability (NEEDS BUILDING)
     - Deterministic scoring using existing qualifyGoodsOpportunity() logic
     - Generates SearchProfile per candidate with query waves
     - Decides: auto-search (≥0.65) / top-value-only (0.45-0.64) / RFQ (<0.45)
     → Result: search_profiles table populated, candidates ranked for search

PHASE D: BUILD MARKETPLACE SEARCH (THE BIG ONE)
────────────────────────────────────────────────
 D1. Stage 9: Price-Signal Match — Alibaba/1688 search skills
     - New agent-driven skills: marketplace search on Chinese platforms
     - Auth skills already exist (auth-refresh/alibaba, auth-refresh/1688)
     - Source health skills exist (source-health/alibaba)
     - Need: product search skill, detail-page scraping skill
     - Multi-wave search with recovery ladder
     - Quote extraction: price, MOQ, shipping, supplier details
     → Result: supplier_quotes table populated with real Chinese supplier prices

PHASE E: BUILD COMMERCIAL INTELLIGENCE
───────────────────────────────────────
 E1. Stage 10: Gross Margin Gate (NEEDS BUILDING)
     - Revenue estimation from contract value + candidate share
     - Quote selection with confidence gating
     - Gross margin computation
     → Result: candidate_pricing_assessments populated

 E2. Stage 11: Landed Cost — screening-grade (NEEDS BUILDING)
     - HMRC Trade Tariff API integration for duty rates
     - Flat freight estimation
     - FX rate from HMRC/Xe
     - VAT computation
     → Result: landed_cost_estimates populated with screening-grade costs

 E3. Stage 12: Net Margin Gate (NEEDS BUILDING)
     - Net margin scenarios (low/base/high)
     - Blocker detection (customs uncertainty, trade remedies, etc.)
     → Result: final go/no-go per candidate

PHASE F: DECISION SURFACE
──────────────────────────
 F1. Stage 13-14: Coverage Rollup + Manifest Generation
     - TypeScript code exists (computeCoverageRollup), needs daemon wiring
     - Manifest generation skill exists, needs real invocation
     → Result: pipeline_assessments + manifests populated, visible in Tauri app

 F2. Stage 15: Operator Decision
     - UI already exists in Tauri (ContractDetail, ContractList views)
     - pending_operator_intents table ready
     → Result: human review and approve/reject flow
```

## Turn 148

### User (2026-04-17T17:09:26)

What parts of the pipeline stages are supposed to be run by runner agents vs pure code based extraction/ analysis?

---

### Assistant (2026-04-17T17:10:25)

From the L0 stage definitions and the codebase:

| Stage | Type | How It Runs |
|-------|------|-------------|
| 1. **Ingest** | **Agent (skill)** | PTY + agent-browser. Navigates procurement portals, scrapes notices. |
| 2. **Normalize** | **Code** | Rust daemon. OCDS field parsing, entity creation. |
| 3. **Relate** | **Code** | TypeScript (`resolveProcessRelations()`) called from Rust. Heuristic scoring, no LLM. |
| 4a. **Prequalify Notice** | **Code** | Rust daemon + TypeScript (`qualifyGoodsOpportunity()`). CPV classification, expiry check, access route filter. |
| 5a. **Acquire Documents** | **Agent (skill)** | PTY + agent-browser. Navigates portals, downloads PDFs/DOCX. |
| 5b. **Structural Extraction** | **Code** | Rust daemon spawns Docling (Python ML subprocess). No LLM, no agent. |
| 5c. **Semantic Extraction** | **Agent (skill)** | Headless contract mode. Claude reads Docling markdown, extracts domain-specific fields (lots, pricing, BoQ). |
| 6. **Extract Lots** | **Code** | Rust daemon. Parses lot structure from notices + semantic extraction output. |
| 4b. **Qualify Lot** | **Code** | Same as 4a but per-lot grain. |
| 7. **Extract Products** | **Agent (skill)** | Reads tender documents + semantic extractions, classifies candidateType/matchArchetype, extracts structured product attributes. |
| 8. **Score Quoteability** | **Code** | Deterministic rules-based scoring. Generates SearchProfile with query waves. |
| 9. **Price-Signal Match** | **Agent (skill)** | PTY + agent-browser. Searches eBay (L1) / Alibaba+1688 (L5), scrapes listings, extracts quotes. |
| 10. **Gross Margin Gate** | **Code** | Revenue estimation, quote selection, gross margin math. |
| 11. **Landed Cost** | **Code** | HMRC Tariff API call + freight + FX + duty computation. |
| 12. **Net Margin Gate** | **Code** | Low/base/high margin scenarios, blocker detection. |
| 13. **Coverage Rollup** | **Code** | TypeScript (`computeCoverageRollup()`). Inline metric aggregation. |
| 14. **Manifest Generation** | **Agent (skill)** | Headless contract mode. Claude renders opportunity manifest from pipeline data. |
| 15. **Operator Decision** | **Manual** | Human via Tauri desktop UI. |

### Summary

**Agent-driven (6 stages):** Ingest, Acquire Docs, Semantic Extraction, Extract Products, Price-Signal Match, Manifest Generation

**Pure code (9 stages):** Normalize, Relate, Prequalify, Extract Lots, Qualify Lot, Score Quoteability, Gross Margin Gate, Landed Cost, Net Margin Gate, Coverage Rollup

**Subprocess (1):** Structural Extraction (Docling — Python ML model, not an LLM agent)

**Manual (1):** Operator Decision

The pattern: agents handle anything requiring browser automation (portals, marketplaces) or semantic reasoning over unstructured text (PDF interpretation, manifest writing). Everything involving math, rules, scoring, or API calls is deterministic code.

## Turn 149

### User (2026-04-17T17:11:57)

why is extract lot and qualify lot not agent based skills?

---

### Assistant (2026-04-17T17:12:42)

Good question. The short answer is: the LLM intelligence for lot extraction already happens in **Stage 5c (Semantic Extraction)**, so Stage 6 is just the deterministic consumer of that output.

The architecture chains it like this:

```
Stage 5c: Semantic Extraction (AGENT)
  └─ pdf-extraction/tender-specification skill
  └─ Claude reads Docling markdown, identifies lot boundaries,
     lot titles, descriptions, requirements, CPV codes, product seeds
  └─ Writes to document_semantic_extractions

Stage 6: Extract Lots (CODE)
  └─ Reads document_semantic_extractions from Stage 5c
  └─ Normalizes into notice_lots table rows
  └─ Creates synthetic whole-notice lot if no lots found
  └─ Pure data transformation — the intelligence already happened
```

So the "smart" lot extraction IS agent-driven — it just lives in Stage 5c, not Stage 6. Stage 6 is the write step.

For **Qualify Lot (4b)**, the current design uses CPV codes (a standardized classification system) plus deterministic rules in the TypeScript `qualifyGoodsOpportunity()` function. CPV divisions 3-44 and 48-49 = goods, 45 = works, 50-98 = services. It's a lookup, not reasoning.

**That said** — your instinct is reasonable. For lots where CPV codes are missing or ambiguous, or where the lot description says "supply and install" (mixed goods+services), a deterministic classifier can't fully resolve it. The L0 doc acknowledges this with `goodsSeparability` and `qualificationBasis` fields that track confidence. The current architecture accepts screening-grade accuracy on classification and defers harder cases to operator review. An agent-based qualifier could handle those edge cases better, but the plan keeps it deterministic for now.

The structural failures are fixable: `.doc` files need conversion to `.docx` before passing to Docling (e.g., via LibreOffice/`soffice --convert-to docx`). The semantic failures should resolve once the runtime blocker fix lands and the structural extractions succeed.

### Assistant (2026-04-17T17:24:46)

Found the root cause. Multiple documents per tender share the same `stage_run_id`. The `process_stage_status.id` is `format!("{stage_run_id}-{state}")` — so when the FIRST document in a tender completes, it writes `stage-run-xxx-completed`. When the SECOND document tries to write the same ID, it hits a `UNIQUE constraint` violation and the job fails.

This is not an extraction problem at all — Docling would succeed on these `.docx` files. The bug is in the stage-status tracking for multi-document tenders.

The table has a unique constraint on `(process_id, stage, scope_kind, scope_id, stage_run_id)`. The scope for structural extraction is `notice` — same scope_id for all docs in one notice. So the unique constraint IS the issue, not the primary key.

Wait, looking again — the `id` field is the PRIMARY KEY, and the `UNIQUE` constraint is separate. The collision is on the PK (`id`), because `format!("{stage_run_id}-{state}")` is the same for multiple documents within the same notice.

But actually, per-notice there should only be ONE `process_stage_status` entry for the `acquire docs` stage — the stage tracks "has this notice's document acquisition succeeded", not "has this individual document been extracted". The problem is that each document extraction job tries to write its own stage status, when really only ONE should write it (or it should use `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`).

Let me fix both issues now. The `process_stage_status` insert should use `ON CONFLICT` to handle multiple documents writing to the same stage entry:

### Assistant (2026-04-17T17:46:48)

Here's what I fixed and what was found:

---

## Summary

### Issue 1: 33 of 37 structural extraction failures — NOT a Docling problem

**Root cause:** `process_stage_status.id` collision. The ID was `format!("{stage_run_id}-{state}")`, which is the same for every document in a multi-document tender. The first document's stage-status INSERT succeeds; every subsequent document in the same tender hits a primary key collision and the job is marked `failed` with "inserting process_stage_status row".

**Fix:** Changed the ID to `format!("{stage_run_id}-{scope_id}-{state}")` (includes scope_id for uniqueness) and added `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` so multiple documents from the same notice safely upsert the same stage entry. File: `crates/daemon/src/agents/contracts.rs` line 208.

### Issue 2: 3 of 37 structural extraction failures — `.doc` format

**Root cause:** Docling supports `.docx` but not legacy binary `.doc` format. Three ProContract tender documents were `.doc` files.

**Fix:** Added `.doc` → `.docx` pre-conversion in `crates/daemon/src/pdf/engine.rs`. Uses macOS `textutil` (always available, tested and confirmed working on the actual failed files) with LibreOffice fallback. Conversion happens in a temp directory before Docling receives the file. Added `tempfile` to Cargo.toml dependencies.

### Issue 3: 1 of 37 structural extraction failures — encrypted PDF

This is a legitimate failure (encrypted PDFs can't be extracted). No fix needed — the error code `EXTRACTION_ENCRYPTED_PDF` is correct.

### Issue 4: All 39 semantic extraction failures — `AGENT_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH`

These are **downstream of the structural failures**. Semantic extraction jobs are enqueued as children of structural extraction jobs. When the parent structural job fails (due to issues 1-3 above), the semantic job still tries to run but the document_extraction it references doesn't exist, causing the protocol mismatch. Once the structural extraction issues are resolved and jobs are re-run, semantic extractions should succeed.

### Build status
- `cargo check --all-targets` — clean
- `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` — clean
- 53 tests pass, 1 pre-existing failure (unrelated `test_role_from_structural_content` in policy.rs)
- Both ProContract persist tests pass

The failed jobs in the DB will need to be retried (reset status from `failed` to `pending`) after these fixes are deployed.

Here's where things stand:

## Fixes Applied This Session

### Fix 1: `process_stage_status` ID collision (contracts.rs)
**Root cause:** 33 of 37 structural extraction failures were NOT Docling failures. The `process_stage_status.id` was `format!("{stage_run_id}-{state}")` — identical for all documents in the same tender. Second document in a multi-document tender hit a PK collision.
**Fix:** Changed ID to `format!("{stage_run_id}-{scope_id}-{state}")` and added `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE`.
**Result:** Structural extractions now complete successfully — 125 completed (up from 12 before fixes), 197 `document_extractions` rows (up from 64).

### Fix 2: Legacy `.doc` format conversion (engine.rs)
**Root cause:** 3 of 37 failures were `.doc` files (legacy binary Word) that Docling can't read.
**Fix:** Added automatic `.doc` → `.docx` conversion via macOS `textutil` (with LibreOffice fallback) before passing to Docling. Tested on actual failed files — conversion works.

### Fix 3: Claude adapter contract-mode parser (claude.rs)
**Root cause:** ALL 39 semantic extraction failures. The Claude CLI in `--output-format stream-json` emits JSONL events with types `system`, `rate_limit_event`, `assistant`, `result`. The daemon parser crashed on non-JSON lines (ANSI codes, progress text) AND on unknown event types. Two bugs:
- Non-JSON lines in contract mode weren't filtered (only PTY mode filtered them)
- Unknown event types like `system`/`assistant`/`rate_limit_event` triggered `bail!`
**Fix:** Added `starts_with(b"{")` guard for contract mode lines (matching the existing Codex adapter pattern), changed unknown event types from `bail!` to `Ok(Vec::new())`, and added `result` event handling for Claude CLI's format (extracts inline result text to temp file).

### Pre-existing: 1 encrypted PDF
Correctly fails with `EXTRACTION_ENCRYPTED_PDF`. Not fixable — the PDF is password-protected.

## Current State

| Item | Count |
|------|-------|
| Structural extractions completed | 125 |
| Structural extractions failed (encrypted) | 1 |
| Structural extractions pending | ~5 (finishing now) |
| `document_extractions` rows | 197 |
| Semantic extractions failed (old binary) | 39 |
| Semantic extractions pending | 68 |
| `document_semantic_extractions` rows | 0 (awaiting retry with new binary) |

## What Happens Next

The background batch is finishing the last structural jobs. Once it completes, the 39 failed semantic jobs need to be reset to `pending` and retried — they should now work with the contract-mode parser fix. The 68 pending semantic jobs in the queue should also succeed.

Once semantic extractions start working, `document_semantic_extractions` will be populated with lot structures, product requirements, and pricing data extracted from the tender documents — unlocking the downstream pipeline stages.

The workdir fallback should catch it. Let me check the order of operations — the `result` event in the stream is being processed BEFORE the workdir fallback:

The flow is:
1. Claude CLI runs, agent writes `result.json` to workdir
2. Claude CLI exits, stream closes
3. Daemon processes stdout → finds `result` event with narrative text → emits `FinalResultReady` with temp file path
4. Daemon checks events for `FinalResultReady` → finds the temp file (narrative text)
5. Daemon tries to parse the temp file as JSON → fails

The fix: the workdir `result.json` should take PRIORITY over the stream event. Let me restructure:

---

## Session Summary — All Fixes Applied, Pipeline Working

### Fixes made this session (5 total):

1. **`process_stage_status` ID collision** (`contracts.rs`) — Changed ID to include `scope_id`, added `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. Fixed 33 structural extraction failures.

2. **Legacy `.doc` → `.docx` conversion** (`engine.rs`) — Auto-converts via macOS `textutil` before Docling. Fixed 3 structural extraction failures.

3. **Claude contract-mode parser** (`claude.rs`) — Skip non-JSON lines in contract mode, handle unknown event types gracefully. Fixed all 39 semantic extraction `AGENT_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH` errors.

4. **`--verbose` flag for contract mode** (`claude.rs`) — Claude CLI requires `--verbose` with `--output-format stream-json` when using `-p`. Fixed "missing result contract path" errors.

5. **`agent_session_logs` upsert** (`main.rs`) — Added `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` so retried jobs don't collide on session ID.

6. **Workdir `result.json` priority** (`main.rs`) — Contract mode now prefers `workdir/result.json` over Claude's stream `result` event (which contains narrative text, not structured JSON).

7. **PDF extraction skill instructions** (4 SKILL.md files) — Added explicit "CRITICAL: Result Contract" section telling the agent to write `result.json` to the workdir.

### Current pipeline state:

## Turn 619

### User (2026-04-18T14:53:30)

Wait, why does pipeline stage 6 happen before stage 3? I thought we have a sequantial pipeline from stage 1-15. Can you explain to me what is going on and how the stages of the pipeline work?

---

### Assistant (2026-04-18T14:53:56)

Good question. The pipeline is NOT strictly sequential 1-15. The L0 doc defines the stage numbers, but the actual execution order has branches and prerequisites. Here's the real flow:

```
 1. Ingest          (fetch notices from sources)
 2. Normalize       (parse into canonical entities)
 
 ┌─── BRANCH A: Qualification ──────────────────────────┐
 │ 4a. Prequalify Notice  (CPV goods/services, expiry)  │
 │       │                                              │
 │       ▼                                              │
 │ 5. Acquire Documents  (download PDFs from portals)   │
 │   5b. Structural Extraction  (Docling)               │
 │   5c. Semantic Extraction    (Claude overlay skills)  │
 │       │                                              │
 │       ▼                                              │
 │ 6. Extract Lots  (refine lots from PDF evidence)     │
 │       │                                              │
 │       ▼                                              │
 │ 4b. Qualify Lot  (per-lot classification)            │
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

 ┌─── BRANCH B: Relations (independent) ────────────────┐
 │ 3. Relate  (dedup/merge across sources)              │
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

 7. Extract Products      (needs: qualified lots + semantic extractions)
 8. Score Quoteability     (needs: product candidates)
 9. Price-Signal Match     (needs: search profiles)
10. Gross Margin Gate      (needs: supplier quotes)
11. Landed Cost            (needs: pricing assessment)
12. Net Margin Gate        (needs: landed cost)
13. Coverage Rollup        (needs: all above)
14. Manifest Generation    (needs: coverage)
15. Operator Decision      (needs: manifest)
```

The key insight: **Stage 4a (Prequalify) happens BEFORE Stage 5 (Acquire Documents)**. The notice-level prequalification is a preliminary gate — it uses only CPV codes from the ingested API metadata to decide if a contract is worth pursuing. If a contract is pure services (CPV 50-98), there's no point downloading and extracting its PDF documents.

Then Stage 6 (Extract Lots) happens AFTER documents are acquired and extracted (Stage 5), because PDF tender documents often contain detailed lot breakdowns not present in the API metadata. Stage 4b (Qualify Lot) then re-qualifies each lot using the richer evidence from the documents.

Stage 3 (Relate) is independent — it can run at any point after normalization. It doesn't depend on qualification or documents.

**The numbering is thematic, not sequential.** 4a and 4b are both "qualification" but run at different points. The actual dependency graph matters more than the stage numbers.

For our current situation: since we already have documents acquired and extracted (Stage 5 is done), and we have 48 lots from ingestion plus 545 product candidates from semantic extraction, we can run 4a, 4b, and 3 — but 4a needs at least one lot per notice for its FK constraint, which is why the `--pipeline` handler creates implicit lots first.

## Issues Found & Fixed

### Issue 1 — CPV extraction misses ProContract’s `categories` field

**Symptom (Check 5 on first run):**
All 103 notices classified as `"unknown"`. Only one category — below the ≥2-category pass bar.

**Root cause:**
`run_prequalify_stage` in `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:9208-9217` read CPV only from the OCDS JSON path `tender.classification.id`. ProContract HTML-scraped payloads are not OCDS — they carry CPV codes in a single `categories` string:

```
"45210000-2 - Building construction work | 45221111-3 - Road bridge construction work"
```

73 / 103 payloads carried this field, but `tender.classification.id` was absent in every payload, so `cpv_division` was always `None` → `"unknown"`.

The handoff prompt anticipated ProContract → "unknown" in general, but the `categories` field was still being discarded even when it contained perfectly valid EU CPV-8 codes. Fixing the extractor is a **bug fix**, not a rule change — we did not touch the CPV-division-to-category mapping or the confidence/density thresholds.

**Fix:**
- Added a module-level helper `parse_cpv_division` that accepts EU CPV-8 codes (8 ASCII digits + optional `-N` check digit) and rejects the 6-digit proprietary codes ProContract emits alongside real CPV (e.g. `150000 - Consultancy`, `171010 - Adult & Further Education Services`). It returns the first 2 digits as the CPV division.
- Updated the extraction chain: OCDS path first; if absent, split `payload_json.categories` on `|`, trim, and pick the first valid CPV-8 code.

File: `crates/daemon/src/main.rs` (`run_prequalify_stage` + new `parse_cpv_division` helper directly above it).

**Verification workflow:**
1. `cargo check --all-targets` — clean.
2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` — clean.
3. `cargo build --release --bin daemon` — clean.
4. Reset `prequalify notice` + `qualify lot` heads/status and cleared `qualification_runs`.
5. Re-ran the pipeline for all three sources.
6. Re-ran every validation query.

Result: 4 categories, 11 goods-classified notices (10 of them also expiry-qualified → PASSED-goods), 40 rejected services/works, 49 review-unknowns, 4 expired.

## Issues Remaining

None blocking Branch A.

### Observations worth noting for the follow-up session

1. **`extract lots` stage head is only written for notices with zero incoming lots** (93 of 103). The other 10 notices already had ≥1 lot carried in by earlier stages and are correctly excluded by the `NOT EXISTS (...)` predicate in `run_extract_lots_stage`. Every exit criterion is satisfied (every notice has ≥1 lot; every lot has a `qualify lot` head), but if downstream code ever expects an `extract lots` head per notice, the query would need to emit a no-op head for already-lotted notices.

2. **51 notices in "REVIEW: unknown"** split into two root causes:
   - 30 payloads have no `categories` field at all (the scraping step dropped it, or the ProContract page didn’t expose one).
   - ~21 payloads carry only 6-digit ProContract proprietary codes (e.g. `150000 - Consultancy`, `171010 - Adult & Further Education Services`) with no EU CPV-8 code. Mapping those proprietary codes to CPV divisions is a future enrichment step (would likely belong in extract-products or an enrichment skill, not in prequalify).

3. **Source filter caveat:** `--pipeline contracts-finder` and `--pipeline fts` both returned all-zeros — there are no notices from those sources in the DB. If FTS/CF ingestion is brought online later, the existing OCDS path will handle them without further changes.

4. **`notice_lots.stage_run_id` link:** the 93 implicit lots were stamped with the first run’s `stage_run_id`, and the 48 pre-existing lots still carry their earlier run IDs. Nothing here breaks Branch A, but joins that use `stage_run_id` to trace a single lot back to a single `extract lots` run will see two populations.

## Turn 650

### User (2026-04-18T15:36:33)

Let's move on to branch B, before we start, can you explain to me exactly what it does?

---

### Assistant (2026-04-18T15:36:56)

Branch B is **Stage 3: Relate** — it detects duplicate or related procurement processes across your data sources.

The problem it solves: the same tender can appear on multiple portals. A contract published on FTS might also appear on Contracts Finder, or a ProContract notice might reference the same procurement as an FTS notice. Without deduplication, you'd analyze and price the same opportunity twice.

**What the current implementation does (OCID matching only):**

It groups all processes by their `canonical_ocid` field. If two or more processes share the same OCID, it creates a `process_relations` entry marking them as `merged_same_process` with 100% confidence. Processes with unique OCIDs (or no OCID) get no relation edges but are still marked as having completed the `relate` stage.

**What it does NOT do yet (L3 scope — full heuristic resolution):**

The TypeScript engine in `packages/intelligence/src/relations/resolve-process-relations.ts` has a much richer resolution engine with:
- Exact rules: same OCID, linked notice chains, framework call-offs
- 6 weighted heuristic features: reference match (0.35), buyer match (0.2), title similarity (0.2), CPV overlap (0.1), date proximity (0.1), value proximity (0.05)
- Thresholds: >=0.95 → merge, 0.75-0.94 → related, <0.75 → separate
- Hard blockers: conflicting references, buyer mismatch, incompatible chronology

This full engine exists in TypeScript but is NOT wired into the daemon's `run_relate_stage()`. The daemon only does the simple OCID matching.

**For our current data:** Since all 103 notices are ProContract-only (zero FTS/CF notices in the DB), and ProContract OCIDs are all unique (`pc-{advertId}`), the Relate stage will likely find **zero duplicates**. Every process gets marked as `relate` completed with no relation edges. This is correct — there's nothing to deduplicate when you have a single source with unique IDs.

Branch B becomes meaningful when we have overlapping data from multiple sources (FTS + CF + ProContract all ingesting the same procurement landscape).

### Assistant (2026-04-18T16:46:12)

## The Commercial Intelligence Pipeline (Stages 7-15)

These stages take the 10 viable goods contracts from Branch A and turn them into priced, margin-calculated opportunities ready for operator decision.

---

### Stage 7: Extract Products

**Input:** Qualified lots + semantic extraction output from tender documents
**Output:** `product_candidates` table — one row per distinct purchasable item

Takes a lot like "Supply of Double Glazed Units" and breaks it down into specific products: "4mm toughened glass pane 1200x600mm", "uPVC frame profile white 70mm", etc. Each candidate gets:
- `head_noun` — the primary product term (e.g., "glass pane")
- `candidate_type` — exact_sku / standard_commodity / brand_family / bundle / custom
- `match_archetype` — how to search for it (standard_class_led / dimension_material_led / model_exact)
- Specifications: quantity, unit, material, dimensions, standards (ISO/EN/BS references)
- Confidence scores: identity, spec, quantity, quoteability

**Current state:** 545 candidates exist from semantic extraction domain writes. May be usable as-is or may need refinement.

---

### Stage 8: Score Quoteability

**Input:** Product candidates from Stage 7
**Output:** `search_profiles` table — one search plan per candidate

A deterministic scoring gate that decides: is this product searchable on a marketplace? It evaluates:
- Does it have a clear commodity noun? (e.g., "steel pipe" yes, "bespoke consultancy" no)
- Are the specs complete enough to match? (dimensions, standards, material specified)
- Is quantity clear?
- Is this a standard off-the-shelf product or custom fabrication?

Produces a **quoteability score**:
- >= 0.65 → auto-search with full wave plan
- 0.45-0.64 → search only for top-value candidates
- < 0.45 → skip marketplace, mark as RFQ-only

For each searchable candidate, generates a `SearchProfile` with **query waves** — progressively broader search strategies (exact match → alias swap → market framing → broad existence).

---

### Stage 9: Price-Signal Match (Marketplace Search)

**Input:** Search profiles from Stage 8
**Output:** `supplier_quotes` table — real supplier prices from marketplaces

**This is the biggest build.** An agent-driven skill that:
1. Takes the query waves from the search profile
2. Navigates to eBay (L1-L2 plan) using agent-browser
3. Executes multi-wave search (wave 1: exact terms, wave 2: aliases, wave 3: broader framing, wave 4: existence check)
4. Scrapes listing results: product title, price, MOQ, shipping, supplier
5. Visits detail pages for promising matches to extract specs, compliance evidence
6. Applies hard-conflict filters (wrong material, wrong size, wrong voltage)
7. Builds an adaptive shortlist (diversity rules: max 2 per seller, 2 per wave)
8. Returns structured `SupplierQuote` records with:
   - Unit price in GBP pence
   - Match confidence + quote usability confidence
   - Shipping basis, lead time, MOQ
   - Price signal role (primary / secondary / reference)

For the 10 viable goods contracts with however many searchable candidates, this produces real Chinese/marketplace supplier prices.

---

### Stage 10: Gross Margin Gate

**Input:** Supplier quotes from Stage 9
**Output:** `candidate_pricing_assessments` table

Deterministic computation:
1. **Revenue estimation:** Take the contract's estimated value, divide by lots, divide by candidate share → expected addressable revenue per candidate
2. **Quote selection:** Pick the best supplier quote using lexicographic gating: identity confidence → usability confidence → realism confidence
3. **Gross margin:** `(revenue - supplier_cost) / revenue`
4. **Gate:** margin >= 30% AND confidence >= 60% → pass

This tells you: "If we bought this product at this price and sold at the contract value, what's the raw margin before duties and shipping?"

---

### Stage 11: Landed Cost (Screening-Grade)

**Input:** Pricing assessments from Stage 10
**Output:** `landed_cost_estimates` table

Calculates the REAL cost of importing the product to the UK:
- **Customs duty:** HMRC Trade Tariff API lookup at HS6 level, MFN rate
- **Freight:** Flat estimate (screening-grade — L5 adds real freight API)
- **FX conversion:** HMRC customs rate + commercial spot rate
- **VAT:** Standard 20% rate (screening-grade — L5 adds recovery modeling)
- **Final mile:** UK delivery estimate
- **Other fees:** Handling, documentation

Produces: `economic_landed_cost_gbp_pence` = supplier + freight + duty + VAT + final mile + fees

Conservative defaults (L0 constitutional): unknown origin → MFN rate, unknown exporter → residual, unknown quota → out-of-quota, unknown VAT → standard rate.

---

### Stage 12: Net Margin Gate

**Input:** Landed cost from Stage 11
**Output:** Final go/no-go per candidate

Computes three scenarios:
- **Low:** worst-case (high freight, high duty, low revenue)
- **Base:** expected case
- **High:** best-case (low freight, favorable duty, high revenue)

`net_margin = (revenue - landed_cost) / revenue`

Checks for **blockers** that prevent a reliable estimate:
- Classification unstable (commodity code uncertain)
- Trade remedy detected (anti-dumping duties)
- Revenue uncertain (contract value missing or ambiguous)
- Customs branch divergent (spread > 2.7% between duty scenarios)

If `margin_low >= 20%` AND no critical blockers → auto-qualify for operator review.

---

### Stage 13: Coverage Rollup

**Input:** All candidates across all lots for a process
**Output:** `pipeline_assessments` table

Aggregates across the full tender:
- **Priced coverage:** what % of lots have at least 1 priced candidate?
- **Landed cost coverage:** what % have a landed cost estimate?
- **Net qualified coverage:** what % passed the net margin gate?
- Value-weighted variants of all three

Decision support bands:
- >= 70% coverage → GREEN (strong opportunity)
- >= 30% → AMBER (partial, needs review)
- < 30% → RED (low coverage, likely not viable)

---

### Stage 14: Manifest Generation

**Input:** Coverage rollup + all pipeline data for a process
**Output:** `manifests` table — human-readable opportunity report

An agent-driven skill (headless contract mode) that renders a markdown opportunity manifest containing:
- Contract summary (buyer, value, deadline, lots)
- Per-lot candidate breakdown with margin bands
- Supplier quote details
- Landed cost scenarios
- Coverage metrics
- Confidence assessment
- Recommended action (pursue / review / skip)

This is what the operator sees in the Tauri desktop app.

---

### Stage 15: Operator Decision

**Input:** Manifest from Stage 14
**Output:** `pending_operator_intents` — approve / reject / watch

The human reviews the manifest in the Tauri desktop app and makes a decision:
- **Approve:** proceed (L6 would automate bid submission)
- **Reject:** not viable, skip
- **Watch:** interesting but not ready, revisit later

The decision captures a `reviewed_basis_hash` so if upstream data changes (new quotes, updated costs), the decision is flagged as stale.

---

### The Dependency Chain

```
 7. Extract Products ──► 8. Score Quoteability ──► 9. Price-Signal Match
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                   10. Gross Margin Gate
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                   11. Landed Cost
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                   12. Net Margin Gate
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                   13. Coverage Rollup
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                   14. Manifest Generation
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                                   15. Operator Decision
```

Strictly sequential — each stage's output feeds the next. The whole chain starts from the 10 PASSED-goods contracts and narrows down to actionable opportunities with real margin data.

### Data-quality observations

- `candidate_type` and `match_archetype` are uniformly `sku` / `direct_match` —
  these are defaults from semantic extraction, not real classifications.
  The Stage 8 scoring therefore de-weights these signals and leans on
  `specification_text` length, `standards_json` presence, head-noun
  well-formedness, and name word count (the dimensions that actually vary
  across the 545 rows).
- All 545 head nouns are single-word (the extractor picked the first noun
  of the phrase). Ten contain a trailing comma (e.g. `stationery,`) — the
  scoring penalises these as malformed.
- The 335 rows with populated `standards_json` sometimes contain legal or
  statutory references instead of technical product standards; Stage 8
  still emits them in wave 2 query text because Stage 9 will judge quality.

## Stage 8: Score Quoteability Results

| Profiles missing `query_waves_json` | 0 |

### Scoring model (deterministic, no LLM)

```
baseline                                      +1000
head_noun ≥4 chars and no comma               +1500   (else +500 if ≥4 chars)
name word count ≥6 / ≥4 / ≥2                  +1500 / +1000 / +500
specification_text length ≥200/≥100/≥50/≥20   +2500 / +1800 / +1000 / +500
standards_json has entries                    +1500
critical_attributes_json has entries          +1000
numeric_specs_json has entries                +1000
quantity present and > 0                       +500
quantity_unit present                          +200
material present                               +500
---
score clamped to [0, 10000]
recall_risk_score_bps = 10000 − quoteability_score_bps
```

Thresholds:
- `eligible` ≥ 6500 bps
- `marginal` ≥ 4500 bps and < 6500 bps
- `ineligible` < 4500 bps

With the current data the theoretical ceiling is 8000 bps
(base + good head + 6-word name + 200+ spec + standards) — no candidate
reached the 10000 cap because structured fields (`quantity`,
`critical_attributes_json`, `numeric_specs_json`, `material`) are almost
uniformly empty.

```
Double-glazed unit (sealed insulating glass unit, BS EN 1279-compliant)

  { "wave": 2, "strategy": "noun_with_spec",  "query": "double-glazed BS EN 1279" },

  { "wave": 4, "strategy": "existence_check", "query": "double-glazed" }
]
```

Wave 2 correctly resolved the standards label (`BS EN 1279`) from
`standards_json`. Every profile has at least one usable wave.

## Issues Found & Fixed

1. **Clippy: `manual_clamp`** — `score.min(10000).max(0)` flagged by pedantic.
   Fixed by using `score.clamp(0, 10_000)` in both scoring helpers.
2. **Clippy: `missing_const_for_fn`** — `eligibility_state_for` was pure and
   could be `const fn`. Fixed by adding the `const` qualifier.
3. **Clippy: `option_if_let_else`** — the wave-2 construction has a multi-arm
   fall-through that doesn't map cleanly to `Option::map_or_else`. Applied
   a local `#[allow(clippy::option_if_let_else)]` on `build_query_waves`.

## Issues Remaining

1. **Upstream extraction gaps** (not blocking Stage 8):
   - All 545 candidates have default `candidate_type=sku`, `match_archetype=direct_match`.
     The extraction skill should classify these from the 6 archetypes defined
     in the search-intelligence plan (direct_match, standards_compliant,
     brand_family, custom_fabrication, bundle, substitute).
   - Structured fields (`critical_attributes_json`, `numeric_specs_json`,
     `quantity`, `material`) are near-empty. These would meaningfully raise
     scores once populated (each adds 500–1000 bps) but do not block Stage 8.
   - `standards_json` occasionally contains statutory or legal references
     rather than product standards. Stage 9 agents will judge wave-2 query
     usability.
2. **Tender document enrichment for 6 contracts** — listed above under
   "Passed-goods contracts without candidates". Out of scope for this
   handoff; tracked separately.

### Assistant (2026-04-19T16:05:55)

Here's the complete picture.

## Stages 9-15: What Each Does

| Stage | Type | What Happens |
|-------|------|-------------|
| **9. Price-Signal Match** | **Agent skill** (browser) | Searches marketplaces for supplier prices. Source routing by candidate type — eBay/Amazon for UK benchmarks, Alibaba/1688 for Chinese wholesale. 5-wave query strategy per candidate (exact → alias → market frame → broad → compliance-off). Outputs `supplier_quotes` with dual confidence scoring and price-signal role (benchmark ceiling vs optimistic floor). |
| **10. Gross Margin Gate** | **Code** | Revenue estimation + quote selection via lexicographic gating (identity → usability → realism). Computes `gross_margin = (revenue - supplier_cost) / revenue`. Gate: margin >= 30% AND confidence >= 60%. |
| **11. Landed Cost** | **Code** + HMRC API | Calculates real import cost: customs duty (HMRC Tariff API), freight, FX (HMRC customs + Xe commercial), VAT, final mile. Constitutional conservative defaults (MFN, residual, out-of-quota, standard VAT). |
| **12. Net Margin Gate** | **Code** | Three scenarios (low/base/high). 8 hard blockers that override margin (classification unstable, trade remedy, customs branch divergent, etc.). Gate: `margin_low >= 20%` AND no blockers. |
| **13. Coverage Rollup** | **Code** (inline) | NOT a tracked stage. Computes coverage metrics: what % of lots are priced/costed/qualified. Value-weighted gate: `netQualifiedValueCoverage >= 50%`. |
| **14. Manifest Generation** | **Agent skill** (L1: markdown, L2: rendered) | Per-process opportunity report with margin bands, candidate details, coverage metrics. |
| **15. Operator Decision** | **Manual** (L2: local, L4: remote) | Human approve/reject/watch in Tauri UI. |

## Chinese Marketplace Search: Where Does It Live?

**Chinese marketplace search IS part of Stage 9, but NOT until Layer 5.** The stage architecture is forward-compatible — the source routing table already maps candidate types to Alibaba/1688:

| Candidate Type | UK Sources | China Sources |
|---|---|---|
| `exact_sku` | eBay, Amazon | Only if equivalent allowed |
| `standard_commodity` | eBay, Amazon | **Alibaba, 1688** |
| `regulated_goods` | eBay, Amazon | **Alibaba, 1688** (with compliance) |
| `brand_family` | eBay, Amazon | Alibaba (if generic exists) |

But **the L1-L3 implementation is eBay-only**. The layer docs are explicit:

> Monorepo layout: `ebay/ (L0)`, `amazon/ (L5)`, `alibaba/ (L5)`, `1688/ (L5)`

So for our current L1-L3 build, Stage 9 searches eBay only. Alibaba, 1688, and Amazon are Layer 5 additions.

## Layer-to-Stage Mapping

This is the critical finding:

```
 LAYER 1 (Thin Local Runner):
   ALL 15 stages introduced and LIVE with real data
   Marketplace: eBay only
   Landed cost: screening-grade (HMRC API + flat freight + single FX)
   Bid workflow: stub (exists but dormant)

 LAYER 2 (Desktop):
   NO pipeline stage changes
   Adds: desktop UI, operator decision surface, Contracts Finder source

 LAYER 3 (Hardening):
   NO pipeline stage changes
   Adds: cron, relation resolution depth, validator dry-run, resource arbiter

 LAYER 4 (Shared Visibility):
   NO pipeline stage changes
   Adds: sync engine, multi-user, validation enforcement

 LAYER 5 (Expanded Commercial Intelligence):
   NO NEW stages — MODIFIES existing stages:
   • Stage 9: adds Alibaba + 1688 + Amazon alongside eBay
   • Stage 11: upgrades to decision-grade (multi-source classification,
     10-digit duty, measure interaction, dual FX, real freight APIs)
   • Stages 7-8: enhanced with 5-source customs classification recall
   All changes populate existing nullable columns — no schema changes

 LAYER 6 (Bid Automation):
   NO NEW stages — ACTIVATES Stage 15 (bid workflow)
   • Full state machine for portal submission
   • L5→L6 readiness gate (no active blockers)
```

## What This Means for Us

**ALL 15 stages should be live in L1-L3.** Layers 4-6 don't add new stages — they modify quality (L5: decision-grade) or activate dormant capabilities (L6: bid automation). The pipeline architecture is complete from L1; later layers improve it.

For our L1-L3 QA completion, stages 9-15 all need to work but at **L1-L3 quality level**:
- Stage 9: eBay only (no Chinese marketplaces — that's L5)
- Stage 11: screening-grade landed cost (no multi-source classification — that's L5)
- Stage 15: local operator queue (no remote CAS — that's L4)

The Chinese marketplace search you asked about is an **L5 enhancement to Stage 9**, not a separate stage. When we get to L5, we'd add Alibaba/1688/Amazon skills to the existing Stage 9 source routing, and upgrade Stage 11 from screening-grade to decision-grade with full duty/freight/FX computation.

# Stage 9 Validation Manifest — Marketplace Search

> Generated: 2026-04-19
> Status: **YELLOW** — all build-time verification GREEN; live-execution
> validation (running the 5 skills against real marketplaces for the 57
> searchable candidates) is a separate operational task that requires PTY
> sessions and API spend, and is not performed within this build session.

## Scope of this handoff

Stage 9 is the most complex pipeline stage: 5 separate agent skills, one per
marketplace, with candidate-driven dispatch and per-source pricing semantics.
The work delivered by this manifest is the **buildable infrastructure** —
skills, daemon wiring, persistence — all the way to the point where running
`target/release/daemon --ingest gem-imports` (or the other 4 sources) will
dispatch a real PTY session and persist real data.

What is NOT delivered here: the ~285 live PTY sessions (57 candidates ×
5 sources) that produce the actual `supplier_quotes` rows. Those runs
consume real-world wallclock time and Claude API spend and are expected to
happen over the operational life of the daemon, not inside a single build
session.

## Source Coverage

| Source | Skill Built | Daemon-registered | Attempts | Quotes Found | Avg Price (pence) |
|--------|-------------|-------------------|----------|-------------|-------------------|
| gem-imports    | YES | YES (`gem-imports`)    | 0 | 0 | n/a (no live runs yet) |
| mx-wholesale   | YES | YES (`mx-wholesale`)   | 0 | 0 | n/a |
| amazon-uk      | YES | YES (`amazon-uk`)      | 0 | 0 | n/a |
| ebay-uk        | YES | YES (`ebay-uk`)        | 0 | 0 | n/a |
| alibaba        | YES | YES (`alibaba-search`) | 0 | 0 | n/a |

### Skill package inventory (25 files, 5 × 5)

Every skill package under `skills/marketplace-search/{source}/` contains:

- `SKILL.md` — frontmatter + playbook (source-specific guidance for the
  sub-Claude running the PTY session)
- `result.schema.json` — JSON schema for the skill's emitted `result.json`
- `checkpoint.schema.json` — JSON schema for the continuation cursor
- `canary.inputs.jsonl` — a one-candidate canary input for rehearsal
- `golden.assertions.yaml` — invariants the validator overlay will check

All 10 schema files parse as valid JSON. All 5 canary inputs parse as valid
JSONL. (See `Data Snapshot` below for the validation commands used.)

### Price-signal role mapping

| Source          | `source_id`     | Registry `kind`        | `price_signal_role`      | `region` | `quote_class` |
|-----------------|-----------------|------------------------|--------------------------|----------|---------------|
| Gem Imports     | `gem-imports`   | `Supplier`             | `uk_wholesale`           | `GB`     | `wholesale`   |
| MX Wholesale    | `mx-wholesale`  | `Supplier`             | `uk_wholesale`           | `GB`     | `wholesale`   |
| Amazon UK       | `amazon-uk`     | `Supplier`             | `uk_retail_benchmark`    | `GB`     | `spot`        |
| eBay UK         | `ebay-uk`       | `Supplier`             | `uk_retail_benchmark`    | `GB`     | `spot`        |
| Alibaba         | `alibaba-search`| `Supplier`             | `cn_supplier_floor`      | `CN`     | `trade`       |

Note: the Alibaba *marketplace-search* source registers as `alibaba-search`
to keep its namespace distinct from the existing `alibaba` canary /
auth-refresh source (which points to `source-health/alibaba` and
`auth-refresh/alibaba`).

## Wholesale Discount Configuration

`DEFAULT_UK_RETAIL_WHOLESALE_DISCOUNT_BPS = 3000` (30 %) is declared in
`crates/daemon/src/main.rs`. The constant is operator-adjustable in the
2000..=4000 bps range without re-scraping. Stage 9 itself never applies this
discount — raw retail prices from Amazon / eBay land in
`supplier_quotes.unit_price_gbp_pence` unchanged and are tagged with
`price_signal_role = "uk_retail_benchmark"`. Downstream margin math
(Stage 10+) multiplies by `(10000 - bps) / 10000` when computing estimated
wholesale from a retail-benchmark quote.
