## §5 Architectural notes

### IPC handler vs CLI helper duality

Phase 2 chose to add new IPC handlers that mirror the CLI helpers' SQL,
rather than refactor the existing CLI helpers in `crates/daemon/src/main.rs`
to take typed args. Reason: the existing 5 Stage-14 daemon-binary tests
(`capture_decision_inserts_pending_operator_intents_row_with_correct_hashes`,
`capture_clarification_validates_payload_per_reason_code`, etc.) call those
helpers with `&Cli`. Refactoring the signatures would have required updating
those tests in lockstep, expanding blast radius for marginal benefit. The
duplication is small (~3 functions × ~30 SQL lines each) and tracked.

The existing `decisions.rs::capture_decision` IPC handler was extended in
place (added `decision_note`); it does NOT share code with `capture_operator_decision`
in main.rs (different idempotency models — IPC uses
`pending_operator_intent_receipts`; CLI uses
`pending_operator_intents.idempotency_key` UNIQUE constraint). This split
predates Phase 2.

### No new daemon migrations

All schema needed by Phase 2 was already in place from Phases 1–14:
`manifests.clarification_prompts_json`, `manifests.stoplight`,
`manifests.suggested_action`, `pending_operator_intents.decision_note`,
`operator_input_overrides.*`. Phase 2 only added queries.

### `trigger_pipeline_rerun`

Implemented as a thin alias for `trigger_enrichment`, which already triggers
the correct sequence (Stage 11 landed cost → Stage 12 net margin gate →
Stage 13 manifest regeneration). The React side polls `manifest_basis_hash`
post-rerun to detect completion, matching the design in §1 D9 of the handoff.

---

## What's left to test — given L1-L3 built, L4-L6 not built

### A. High-value, doable today

1. **Re-run quality gates on freshly merged main.** All 4 handoffs were validated independently against different baselines; the merged state hasn't been formally re-gated as a single unit. `cargo fmt --check && cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings && cargo test -p daemon --bin daemon && for t in crates/daemon/tests/*.rs; do cargo test --test "$(basename "$t" .rs)" || break; done`.

2. **Live `bun tauri dev` walkthrough** — Stage 14 Phase 2 R.2/R.8/R.10 explicitly deferred. You drive the 9-step demo manually, capture screenshots. This is the only thing standing between Phase 2 being "code-complete" and "operationally proven."

3. **End-to-end golden journey on a real process.** Pick one ProContract process (e.g., DGU) and walk all 14 stages back-to-back, capturing every transition. Existing manifests covered each stage in isolation but not as a continuous narrative. This is what stakeholders ask to see.

4. **Stage 11 CDP Scenarios A & B** (R.10 partial). Requires a CDP browser running on `:9222`. With it: drive `--ingest landed-cost` once with no captcha (Scenario A populates a freight value), then once with `--job-parameters-json '{"simulate_captcha":true}'` (Scenario B should hit the cache). Without CDP, these stay deferred.

### B. Pre-existing flake cleanup (tech debt, not new gaps)

5. **`fault_process_cleanup` 3 timing-sensitive sub-tests** (`_003`, `_004`, `_008`). PTY child cleanup verdict assertions sensitive to OS scheduling. Bead: `MS-FLAKY-PROCESS-CLEANUP`.

6. **`native_shell` 4 pre-existing failures** (`tau_int_003/007/008/010`). Schema-epoch mismatch — fixture uses `schemaEpoch: 0`/`1`, prod constant is now `EXPECTED_SCHEMA_EPOCH = 2`. Update the test welcome-message fixture.

### C. Stale-detection end-to-end (the demo nobody's run)

7. **Cross-session staleness propagation.** Capture a decision via Phase 2 UI → externally trigger `--ingest manifest` (which bumps `manifest_basis_hash`) → reload UI → confirm yellow stale banner appears AND the row's `pending_operator_intents.status` flipped to `'stale'`. Unit tests cover the SQL; the visual + IPC round-trip hasn't been demonstrated.

### D. Multi-source — flow CF data further down the pipeline

8. **CF process → Stages 8-14 walkthrough.** Bead `MS-ENRICH-1` notes CF processes halt at Stage 7 because there's no CF-equivalent of `procontract-enrich-tender`. You could *manually* run the existing `pdf-extraction/*` skills against one CF notice's `tender.documents[]` URLs to walk one CF process through to a manifest. That validates Stages 8-14 work on non-procontract data without building the auto-trigger.

### E. Documentation / hygiene

9. **Strategy docs are stale w.r.t. L1-L3 implementation status.** MEMORY.md mentions "L4-L6 not yet started" but the strategy docs in `docs/strategy/` were written before Stages 11-14 landed. A pass through `docs/strategy/L1.md`, `L2.md`, `L3.md` to mark "implemented" vs "planned" would prevent future agents from acting on stale designs.

---

## What is genuinely NOT testable until L4-L6 are built

- **Stage 15 (bid_workflow)** — L6 commercial intelligence layer.
- **Validator skills + run manifests as first-class artifacts** — L4 validation pipeline. The hooks are present in some places (skill metadata can declare `result_schema_version`, jobs can have `parent_job_id`/`validation_required` columns), but no validator skill executes today.
- **Push/pull sync to Supabase, transactional outbox, CAS for shared decisions** — L5 sync engine. Today everything is local SQLite (`state/state.db`).
- **Multi-org / multi-instance coordination, active-writer lease, schema-epoch transition protocol** — L4-L5.
- **Cron-based scheduled execution as the production driver** — partially scaffolded (`cron_scheduler` integration test passes) but not the production trigger today.

---

If you want one thing to do next: **#2 (live tauri dev walkthrough)** — it's the smallest unit of work that converts a deferred R-criterion into GREEN, and produces shareable artifacts (screenshots) that prove Stage 14 is operator-ready. **#1 (quality gates re-run)** is fast and de-risks the merged state.

Beyond that, **#3 (golden journey)** is the demo you'll show stakeholders to prove "the L1-L3 product works end-to-end on real procurement data." Everything else is polish or L4-L6 territory.

## Design notes

**Validate/test/fix loop in each:** every handoff opens with a `THE LOOP` ASCII block and ends with R.* acceptance criteria + failure-mode playbook + mandatory validation manifest. The loop shape varies by handoff type:
- #1: run gates → diagnose → fix → re-run
- #2: drive UI → screenshot → validate → fix bug → repeat
- #3: query DB → write narrative → validate completeness → refine (discover/write/validate)
- #4: launch CDP → drive scenario → validate → fix or file → repeat
- #5: reproduce → diagnose → fix → run 50× to verify stability
- #6: drive stage → check output → fix narrowly OR document → continue
- #7: read → annotate → cross-reference verify → refine
- #8 (deferred): full build/test/fix loop scoped per L6's eventual templates

**Parallel-safety map:**
- Group A (run together — read-only or non-overlapping): #1, #3, #5, #7
- Group B (sequential — exclusive desktop GUI): #2 then #4
- Group C (sequential — daemon-state-mutating): #6 alone (touches state.db; conflicts with #2/#4)
- Stage 15 (#8): isolated via worktree when run

**Stage 15 specifically respects "do not run yet":**
- Title prefixed `-DEFERRED` so it sorts visibly
- ⛔ marker block at the top before the standard MISSION
- §0 pre-flight has explicit L4/L5/L6-built checks with "STOP" branches
- D2 enforces operator review gate (no autonomous real-money submissions)
- D8 defaults `STAGE15_PORTAL_MODE=simulate`; production requires `--i-understand-this-is-real` flag
- BWF.5 + BWF.6 unit tests assert simulate-mode safety

**Recommended execution order if running soon:**
1. #5 (test flake cleanup) → unblocks the 39/39 + 15/15 baseline
2. #1 (post-merge gate sweep) → confirms steady state after #5
3. #7 (strategy docs hygiene) → low risk, parallelizable
4. #6 (CF Stages 8-14) → proves source-agnostic L1-L3
5. #2 (Phase 2 live UI) → resolves deferred R-criteria
6. #4 (CDP live demos) → resolves deferred R-criteria + may unblock DGU
7. #3 (golden journey) → final stakeholder artifact, benefits from all the above
8. #8 (Stage 15) → after L6 ships, possibly months out

## Handoff delivered: `prompts/full-pipeline-end-to-end-validation-handoff.md` (992 lines)

This is the comprehensive E2E test the user asked for — designed to be one-shot for an agent.

### What it covers

**End-to-end flow** (everything you listed):
- Find contracts → `--ingest procontract` + `--ingest contracts-finder` (live CDP browser + OCDS API)
- Express interest → `--ingest procontract-interest` (REAL portal mutation, capped at ≤3 actions)
- Wait for reply → 3 documented strategies (auto-grant, Apr 21 carryover, real-time pause)
- Get information → `--ingest procontract-enrich` + PDF extraction skills
- Find quotes → iterate 5 marketplaces until ≥1 GBP quote OR document why
- Calculate logistics/customs/FX → `--ingest landed-cost` (HMRC + sizing + SeaRates + FX chain)
- Net margin gate + manifest + operator decision (CLI + UI)
- Desktop UI walkthrough with screenshots
- Cross-session staleness E2E
- Operational subsystem checks (cron, source health, resource arbiter, validation skeleton)

**Scope explicitly bounded**:
- IN: L1-L3 + Stages 1-14 (everything built)
- OUT: L4-L6 (web portal, sync, validators, commercial intelligence), Stage 15 (L6-blocked), real-money actions

### Key design decisions baked in

- **D1 — Isolated state root** (`state-e2e-<timestamp>`): the test does NOT touch live `state/state.db`; sha256sum integrity check at end (R.25)
- **D2 — Spotlight selection** with concrete SQL: fresh procontract notice, goods-like, £5k–£1M, picked after Stage 1
- **D3 — Wait strategies** (3 documented options) so the async portal-approval reality doesn't block the test
- **D4 — Real-mutation safety**: register-interest is the ONLY authorized real action, capped at 3, every notice documented
- **D6 — Bug-fix scope**: small/medium fix in scope with regression test, large gaps go to beads — the validate/test/fix loop is explicit

### Loop pattern

```
For each Stage N:
  drive → validate (DB query + filesystem check) → diagnose any break →
  fix bug in code → add regression test → re-drive Stage N → continue to N+1
```

30 R.* acceptance criteria. Stage-by-stage protocol with trigger commands, expected outcomes, validation queries, and failure-mode tables for every stage. Validation manifest template included. The agent gets the script (§7), the queries (per stage), the deliverable shape (§10), and the failure playbook (§8).

### What an agent needs running before they pick this up

- CDP browser on `:9222`, signed into ProContract
- claude + codex + agent-browser + docling on PATH
- main branch clean (currently is)
- ~4 hours+ depending on wait strategy

The handoff explicitly prompts the operator if any of these aren't satisfied at pre-flight rather than failing silently mid-run.

## Handoff delivered: `prompts/full-pipeline-end-to-end-revalidation-handoff.md` (964 lines)

This is a focused **re-drive** of the previous full-pipeline E2E, designed to confirm every fix landed cleanly without manually re-checking each one.

### What it tests differently from the previous handoff

**Same scope** (L1-L3, Stages 1-14, all built surfaces) — but with **R.31-R.40 as fix-verification gates** layered on top of the original R.1-R.30. After each stage's functional validation, the agent ALSO checks the specific bug that was fixed for that stage.

**Specific fix verifications** baked into the chain:

| Stage | Fix to verify | Regression check |
|---|---|---|
| Stage 4a | Interest cap (a80d2e1) | ≤3 distinct registrations this run |
| Stage 4b | Enrich ID resolution (a80d2e1) | No FK errors in agent_session_logs |
| Stage 8 | Semantic chunking (a80d2e1) | Large PDFs have `docling-markdown.md` (focused) + `-full.md` |
| Stage 8 | Quantity persistence (3845b1f) | `product_candidates.quantity` populated when source had hint |
| Stage 9 | Marketplace coverage orchestration (3845b1f) | Both `uk_side_quote` AND `china_import_quote` rows in `candidate_quote_coverage` |
| Stage 9 | PTY supervisor (a80d2e1) | `cleanup_verdict` ∈ {clean, terminated_after_result_contract} |
| Stage 10 | `commercial_basis_kind` labelling (3845b1f) | Honest label, not `legacy_single_quote` unless intentional |
| Stage 11 | **The "42.39 boilers" check** (3845b1f) | `basis_qty` is integer OR `basis_undetermined` — never fractional from whole-lot/unit-quote when allocated revenue used |
| Stage 11 | Stage 11/10 commercial basis consistency | `confidence_breakdown.commercial_basis.kind` mirrors Stage 10 |
| Stage 11 | Contract-mode timeout (a80d2e1) | HMRC/SeaRates/sizing/FX never hang past `CONTRACT_SKILL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` |
| §4 UI | Choice clarification reason field (a80d2e1) | Submit succeeds for `un_pursueable`/`defer` variants |

### Critical pre-flight gate I added

**Schema epoch alignment**. Right now there's a real footgun:
- Daemon `DEFAULT_SCHEMA_EPOCH = 8` (`crates/daemon/src/db/sqlite.rs:73`)
- Desktop `EXPECTED_SCHEMA_EPOCH = 7` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs:51` + `daemon_bridge.rs:16`)

The previous E2E run hit this exact mismatch mid-flight when a concurrent rebuild bumped the daemon. The new handoff makes this the **FIRST FIX** before the run starts. The agent inspects + bumps + commits + re-runs vitest before proceeding.

### Operational armor against the previous footgun

Per D1, the daemon is built to a **dedicated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`** and pinned via `$E2E_DAEMON`. Per §4, the desktop is launched with `PROCINTEL_DAEMON_PROGRAM=$E2E_DAEMON` so the third-fallback launcher cannot rebuild from cargo workspace mid-run. The footgun is eliminated by construction, not just by warning.

### Updated baselines

- Daemon binary tests: **202 / 202** (was 180; +22 commercial-basis regression tests)
- vitest: **44 / 44** (was 43; +1 for choice clarification reason field)
- Per-file integration: 39/39, query_service 25/25, daemon_bridge 14/14, native_shell 22/22

### Reference points for the new agent

- Previous E2E manifest at `docs/pipeline/full-pipeline-e2e-validation-manifest.md` (29KB) — the canonical "what was tested + what bugs surfaced"
- Commercial-basis follow-up at `docs/pipeline/full-pipeline-e2e-commercial-basis-followup-manifest.md` — the canonical "what state machine was added"
- Previous E2E state root `state-e2e-20260504T113157Z` still on disk as forensic reference (Woodhouses spotlight history) — the new run uses a fresh root, not this one

## Audit summary — what I caught + fixed

This pass found **5 substantive bugs** in the handoff that would have sent the agent down dead ends. The validate/test/fix loop pattern (verify-against-code, fix, recheck) caught them in 3 commits.

### Bug 1 — `--ingest gross-margin` does NOT exist (build-breaker for the agent)

I cited it in 3 places. Verified against `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:1304-1320`: actual dispatcher arms are `fts`, `contracts-finder`, `procontract`, `procontract-interest`, `procontract-enrich`, `landed-cost`, `net-gate`, `manifest`, `marketplace-coverage`, and the 5 marketplace IDs. **No `gross-margin` arm.**

The earlier commercial-basis follow-up manifest (committed by another agent) recommended `--ingest gross-margin` as a command — that was aspirational/wrong. Fixed by:
- Replacing the §3 Stage 10 trigger with `--pipeline procontract` (which calls `run_gross_margin_gate_stage` at `main.rs:21062` as part of `run_deterministic_pipeline`)
- Adding an explicit IMPORTANT callout in the Stage 10 protocol
- Adding a row to the failure-mode playbook explaining why retrying that command will keep failing

### Bug 2 — Wrong `commercial_basis_kind` enum values (R.37 unverifiable as written)

I cited `single_side_with_attempted_alternative` and `fallback_after_import_failure`. Verified against `main.rs:238-241`: actual constants are:
- `two_sided_supplier_comparison`
- `single_side_retail_discount_fallback`
- `single_side_wholesale_fallback`
- `missing_required_quote_role_attempt`
- `legacy_single_quote` (default per migration 0011)

Fixed in 4 places: MISSION, Stage 10 commercial-basis check, R.37, and D12 fix-verification table.

### Bug 3 — Stage 12/13 reasons cited approximately, not exactly

The handoff listed reason strings informally. Replaced with the actual `REASON_*` constants at `main.rs:7508-7522` so the agent's grep against the daemon source verifies cleanly. Examples landed: `missing_required_quote_role_attempt`, `missing_china_import_quote`, `fallback_screening_commercial_basis`, `incomplete_quote_coverage`, etc.

### Bug 4 — Spotlight selection D2 query would return 0 rows on procontract data

The previous CF walkthrough manifest documented that `contract_notices.estimated_value_gbp_ex_vat_pence` is NULL for most procontract notices (value lives in `normalized_payload.tender.value`). My D2 query filtered by the table column, which would zero-row. Added a fallback query that COALESCEs from `normalized_payload` so the agent can find candidates.

### Bug 5 — Pre-flight row claimed `--ingest gross-margin` is wired

Last leftover from Bug 1. Rephrased the pre-flight check so the agent's grep proves the absence rather than the presence — turning a false-positive into a verification gate.

- `MAX_PROCONTRACT_INTEREST_REGISTRATIONS_PER_RUN = 3` (`main.rs:1019`) ✓

Pushed (`927b830`). Synced 0/0. Handoff at 1004 lines, 40 unique R criteria.

## Second-pass audit summary

This pass found **5 substantive bugs** my first audit missed by going deeper into operational behaviors.

### 1. Schema-epoch fix scope was understated by 6 locations (build-breaker risk)

I cited **2 locations** to bump (`main.rs:51`, `daemon_bridge.rs:16`). Verified against the codebase: the constant `7` is hard-coded in **8 locations**:
- 3 source files (`main.rs:51`, `query_service.rs:14`, `daemon_bridge.rs:16`)
- 2 native_shell test fixture files (`tests/support/native_shell.rs:20`, `tests/native_shell.rs:21`)
- 4 `"schemaEpoch": 7` JSON values across 3 Tauri test files (`tests/query_service.rs:42`, `tests/daemon_bridge.rs:59,120,209`)

If the agent had bumped only the 2 source constants, all 4 Tauri test suites would fail — exactly the kind of mid-flight regression that derailed the previous E2E run. Updated pre-flight to enumerate all 8 + provide the discovery command.

### 2. `cleanup_verdict` values DIFFER between PTY and contract-mode skills (silent data corruption risk)

The PTY producer at `main.rs:14917` emits `clean / terminated_after_result_contract / terminated_after_timeout`. The contract-mode producer at `main.rs:11307` emits `clean / terminated_after_result (NO _contract suffix) / terminated_after_timeout`. I had conflated them. R.36 (PTY-only) and R.40 (contract-only) now use the right value sets per skill mode, with explicit code-line citations.

### 3. `marketplace-coverage` BAILS at first failure (operational footgun)

Per `main.rs:16240-16247`, the orchestration iterates `amazon-uk → ebay-uk → gem-imports → mx-wholesale → alibaba-search` and **stops at the first non-completed report**. If amazon-uk hits captcha, alibaba-search never runs — but R.35 still requires a `china_import_quote` row. Added explicit operational note + recovery path (drive failed marketplace individually, then retry coverage).

### 4. `RUN_START_AT` referenced but never defined

Multiple queries used `<run start ISO timestamp>` placeholders without defining how to capture it. Added `export RUN_START_AT="$(date -u -Iseconds)"` to D1 setup + threaded the env var into the queries with a fallback default.

### 5. R.34 (Stage 8 quantity persistence) was vague

Said "if any spotlight source document has explicit quantity hints, `product_candidates.quantity` should be populated". Made concrete: check `quantity IS NOT NULL` AND `quantity_confidence_bps > 5000`. Cited the observed default bands (9200/8000/7800/1000) so the agent has a numeric threshold. The Woodhouses regression specifically had high `quantity_confidence_bps` (~8500) but NULL `quantity` — the new check distinguishes "fixed" from "regressed" precisely.

## Cumulative across both audit passes

**10 substantive bugs caught and fixed** in 2 audit passes:

- Pass 1 (5 bugs): `--ingest gross-margin` doesn't exist (3 places), wrong `commercial_basis_kind` enum values, vague Stage 12/13 reasons, spotlight D2 query won't match procontract data, leftover stale citations
- Pass 2 (5 bugs): schema epoch fix scope understated by 6 locations, PTY vs contract-mode `cleanup_verdict` divergence, marketplace-coverage bail-at-first-failure behavior, undefined `$RUN_START_AT`, vague R.34 quantity check

The handoff is now grounded in 18 verified code-line citations (`main.rs:1019/1020/1025/1026/1304-1320/2111/4099-4108 etc.`) and three test-fixture file paths. Every claim about how the code behaves has a `grep`-able anchor. The agent picking it up cold has no dead ends, no ambiguous values, and an explicit recovery path for the most likely operational failure (marketplace-coverage bailing at amazon-uk).

### Assistant (2026-05-04T21:10:45)

Pushed (`02b3220`). Synced 0/0. Handoff at 1021 lines, 40 unique R criteria.

## Third-pass audit summary

This pass found **5 substantive bugs** missed by passes 1+2 — all about operational realities the agent needs to know to not waste hours.

### 1. Stage 8 PDF extraction is AUTO-TRIGGERED by Stage 4b (biggest miss)

Verified at `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:15846` and `:15892`: `run_procontract_enrich_tender_live_loop` calls `drain_pending_extraction_jobs(runtime, cli, &extraction_process_ids)` automatically after enrichment. My handoff had the agent **manually queuing PDF jobs as the primary path** — that's only the fallback.

Updated Stage 8 protocol: (a) verify auto-trigger ran via `document_extractions` query, (b) only fall back to manual `--skill-path` or `--run-next-job` if auto-trigger silently produced 0 rows or left a stuck `pending` row. Saves the agent at least an hour of confused debugging.

### 2. Why `--pipeline procontract` runs multiple times (was unexplained)

Verified at `main.rs:21068+`: `run_gross_margin_gate_stage` has an `INNER JOIN supplier_quotes ON sq.product_candidate_id = pc.id`. It only processes candidates that already have at least one supplier_quote row.

So the chain is: first `--pipeline procontract` (after Stage 1-3) → 0 gross-margin rows because no quotes yet → Stage 9 marketplace-coverage adds quotes → re-run `--pipeline procontract` → gross-margin-gate finally processes them. Added explicit "Why this is run multiple times" callout so the agent doesn't think the first run is broken when it shows `margins_assessed=0`.

### 3. Schema-epoch bump has POST-RUN consequences (operational landmine for future sessions)

Verified at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs:884-892`: if epoch bumped to 8, desktop refuses any state.db at epoch 7 with `SchemaMismatch`. Live `state/state.db` is at epoch 7 (migration 0011 not yet applied to it).

Added constraint #6 documenting:
- The post-run side effect (live DB becomes desktop-unreadable without env overrides)
- The migration recipe: `target/debug/daemon --state-root state --dry-run` applies pending migrations on first connect
- A mandate to **document this in the validation manifest's operational notes** so future sessions don't think the live DB is broken

### 4. What if Stage 8 produces 0 product_candidates? (edge-case handling)

Documented honestly: pipeline still runs but Stages 9-13 will produce 0 downstream rows. Common causes (boilerplate-only PDFs, no extractable products, service contract). Operator can pick a different spotlight if R.11+ require non-zero. Without this, the agent might thrash trying to "fix" a non-bug.

### 5. Time budget expectation set explicitly (was missing entirely)

Added per-stage breakdown: total **3-8 hours** per spotlight depending on D3 wait strategy. Stage 9 marketplace-coverage alone could be 30-150 min (5-30 min × 5 marketplaces). Stage 11 landed-cost 5-45 min depending on import vs domestic. Without this, an agent might allocate 30 min and abandon when stage 9 alone takes longer.

## Cumulative across 3 audit passes

**15 substantive bugs caught and fixed**:

- **Pass 1 (5)**: `--ingest gross-margin` doesn't exist (cited 3 places), wrong `commercial_basis_kind` enum values, vague Stage 12/13 reasons, spotlight D2 query won't match procontract data, leftover stale citations
- **Pass 2 (5)**: schema epoch fix scope understated by 6 locations, PTY vs contract-mode `cleanup_verdict` divergence, marketplace-coverage bail-at-first-failure behavior, undefined `$RUN_START_AT`, vague R.34 quantity check
- **Pass 3 (5)**: Stage 8 PDF auto-trigger from Stage 4b, why `--pipeline procontract` runs multiple times, schema-epoch post-run consequences, 0-candidates edge case handling, time budget expectations

The handoff is now grounded in **23 verified code-line citations** spanning daemon main.rs (lines 1019-21068), desktop main.rs (51, 884-892), daemon_bridge.rs (16), 3 Tauri test files, and 5 distinct migrations. Every operational claim has a `grep`-able anchor. The agent picking it up cold has:
- No dead-end commands
- No ambiguous values (every enum string is verified)
- An explicit recovery path for the most likely operational failure
- Time budget expectations
- Edge-case handling for the "0 candidates" case
- Post-run housekeeping documented to prevent confusing the NEXT session

## What's genuinely solid

**12 of 12 prior-run fixes confirmed under live load** — that's the load-bearing finding. The "42.39 boilers" regression specifically does NOT recur (R.38), Stage 10 honestly labels its commercial basis (R.37 = `single_side_retail_discount_fallback`, not `legacy_single_quote`), Stage 9 produces both `uk_side_quote` AND `china_import_quote` coverage rows (R.35), and the bounded PTY supervisor fired on 10/10 sessions (R.36). The schema-epoch footgun got fixed in pre-flight (commit `c5d2b88`) before the run even started.

The new commercial-basis state machine works end-to-end against fresh data — Stage 8 persists explicit quantities (3 boilers, conf=8500), Stage 9 attempts both quote roles, Stage 10 labels honestly, Stage 11 refuses unsafe inference with `revenue_cost_scale_mismatch` reason, Stage 12-13 surface `fallback_screening_commercial_basis` + `missing_china_import_quote` in operator-readable form.

## The three deferrals — how worried to be

| Deferral | My read |
|---|---|
| **R.40 Stage 11 import path (HMRC/SeaRates/sizing/FX)** not exercised because spotlight resolved to domestic eBay | **Not blocking.** The CDP live-demos manifest (`c019eec`) already drove this chain end-to-end with real SeaRates pricing in Scenarios A & B. Code-level verification this run plus prior live verification = covered. The 14 contract-mode PDF sessions including 1 actual 600s timeout firing prove the timer mechanism works. |
| **§4 UI click-through screenshots (R.17-R.22)** deferred because Tauri dev binary can't be allowlisted by macOS computer-use without a release `.app` bundle | **Not blocking.** The previous E2E run already drove real mouse clicks against the UI (per `full-pipeline-e2e-validation-manifest.md`). This run verified the bridge handshake at the new schema epoch (`schemaEpoch:8 ready:true`) — that's the load-bearing prerequisite. R.20's substance was exercised through the same daemon CLI the UI wraps. Capturing the screenshots remains a nice-to-have operational artifact. |
| **Qualifier "Not Elsewhere Classified" rejection** — boiler schools score `goods_qualified=0`, requiring manual SQL override to exercise R.35 | **This is the one concern worth acting on before L4 ships.** Details below. |

## The NEC qualifier gap is the one that matters

The manifest treats this as "pre-existing, not introduced by this run's commits" — that's accurate. But it's a **silent data-quality bug** that L4-L6 will inherit and amplify if not fixed first:

- **L4 (Shared Visibility)** pushes local data to Supabase. If the qualifier silently drops a class of legitimate goods opportunities (everything categorized as "Not Elsewhere Classified" — which apparently includes the entire Oldham Council boiler-replacement batch), the web viewing portal will systematically under-show real opportunities. Operators won't know what they're missing.
- **L5 (Commercial Intelligence)** learns from what flows through. A skewed training set (CPV-classified goods only, no NEC goods) skews every downstream judgment.
- **L6 (Request Pipeline Automation)** only triggers on qualified + approved candidates. Silent skipping is worse than visible rejection — the operator gets no signal that an opportunity was rejected pre-qualification.

The gap itself is **bounded** (text-classification fallback when CPV division is unknown) and the manifest already filed it as a follow-up. I'd ship that follow-up as a small handoff in parallel with L4 build start, not block L4 on it.

## What this run did NOT prove

To be honest about the gaps:

1. **No live import-customs landed-cost chain in this specific run.** The spotlight resolved to all-domestic. Coverage exists from prior runs, but a fresh L1-L3 demo on an import-side spotlight would be a stronger reference.
2. **No live operator-driven UI click-through this run.** Bridge handshake = yes; mouse-click clarification + decision = previous run.
3. **The qualifier rejected the natural target type** (boiler procurements, NEC-categorized) without the manual SQL override.

None of these break the architectural foundation L4 needs. But they're honest gaps in the operator-experience proof.

## My recommendation

**Yes, start L4.** Specifically:

1. **Begin L4 architecture work immediately** — the foundations it needs (schema stability, IPC bridge correctness, commercial-basis honesty, quantity safety) are all verified.
2. **In parallel, write a small handoff to fix the NEC qualifier gap** — text-classification fallback when CPV is unknown. This should be a 1-2 day fix, ideally landing before L4's first push of data to Supabase. Otherwise L4's web portal launches with a known data-quality bug.
3. **Schedule a one-shot UI click-through follow-up** to capture the deferred screenshots once you have a release `.app` bundle. Low priority; existing prior evidence is sufficient for shipping.
4. **Optional**: pick a future spotlight that's a Chinese-import-side procurement (e.g., furniture, ICT, lab supplies from Alibaba) for a fresh full-import-chain E2E. Not required for L4 start; useful before L5 since L5 leans heavily on the commercial-readiness state machine.

The L1-L3 product is operationally credible. The validation manifest's GREEN headline is appropriate, even with the deferrals. The only thing that would make me say "wait" would be evidence of architectural instability in the data flow — and there is none.

The 15 substantive bugs the audit passes caught and fixed in handoffs, plus the 12 production fixes the actual E2E runs surfaced and shipped, plus the commercial-basis state machine landing cleanly — that's a maturing foundation. Time to build on it.

Want me to draft the NEC qualifier fix handoff?

## Turn 1371

### User (2026-05-05T08:52:02)

Export Current Session Conversation Log                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                                                                   
  Export the full conversation history from this Claude Code session to a readable markdown file. The /export command only captures what's visible in the terminal — I need the    
  COMPLETE conversation from the JSONL file.                                                                                                                                       
   
  Steps                                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                  
  1. Find the current session's JSONL file (the most recently modified .jsonl in ~/.claude/projects/ for this project directory)
  2. Parse it to extract all user and assistant message entries
  3. Format as a markdown document with numbered turns, timestamps, and full message content                                                                                       
  4. Write to docs/session-log-YYYY-MM-DD-<short-description>.md
                                                                                                                                                                                   
  Script                                                          

  Run this Python script, adjusting the session_file path and output_file name:                                                                                                    
   
  python3 << 'PYEOF'                                                                                                                                                               
  import json, os, glob                                           
  from datetime import datetime

  # Auto-detect: find the most recently modified JSONL for this project
  project_dir = os.path.expanduser("~/.claude/projects/")
  cwd = os.getcwd().replace("/", "-")                                                                                                                                              
  project_sessions = os.path.join(project_dir, cwd)
  jsonl_files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(project_sessions, "*.jsonl")), key=os.path.getmtime, reverse=True)                                                                   
                                                                                                                                                                                   
  if not jsonl_files:
      print("No session files found"); exit(1)                                                                                                                                     
                                                                  
  session_file = jsonl_files[0]
  session_id = os.path.basename(session_file).replace(".jsonl", "")
  print(f"Using session: {session_id} ({os.path.getsize(session_file) / 1024:.0f} KB)")                                                                                            
   
  output_file = f"docs/session-log-{datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d')}.md"                                                                                                       
  os.makedirs("docs", exist_ok=True)                              
                                                                                                                                                                                   
  entries = []                                                    
  with open(session_file, 'r') as f:
      for line in f:
          if line.strip():
              try: entries.append(json.loads(line))
              except: pass                                                                                                                                                         
   
  conversation = []                                                                                                                                                                
  for e in entries:                                               
      msg_type = e.get('type')
      if msg_type == 'user':
          message = e.get('message', {})
          content = message.get('content', '') if isinstance(message, dict) else str(message)
          if isinstance(content, list):                                                                                                                                            
              content = '\n'.join(p.get('text', '[non-text]') if isinstance(p, dict) else str(p) for p in content)
          if content.strip():                                                                                                                                                      
              conversation.append({'role': 'user', 'content': content.strip(), 'ts': e.get('timestamp', '')})
      elif msg_type == 'assistant':                                                                                                                                                
          message = e.get('message', {})                          
          if isinstance(message, dict):                                                                                                                                            
              parts = [b.get('text', '') for b in message.get('content', []) if isinstance(b, dict) and b.get('type') == 'text']
              content = '\n'.join(parts)                                                                                                                                           
          else:                                                                                                                                                                    
              content = str(message)
          if content.strip():                                                                                                                                                      
              conversation.append({'role': 'assistant', 'content': content.strip(), 'ts': e.get('timestamp', '')})

  with open(output_file, 'w') as f:                                                                                                                                                
      f.write(f"# Session Log\n\n**Session:** {session_id}\n**Exported:** {datetime.now().isoformat()}\n\n---\n\n")
      turn = 0                                                                                                                                                                     
      for entry in conversation:                                  
          ts = ''
          if entry['ts']:                                                                                                                                                          
              try:
                  if isinstance(entry['ts'], str): ts = f" ({entry['ts'][:19]})"                                                                                                   
                  else: ts = f" ({datetime.fromtimestamp(entry['ts']/1000 if entry['ts']>1e12 else entry['ts']).strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')})"
              except: pass                                                                                                                                                         
          if entry['role'] == 'user':
              turn += 1                                                                                                                                                            
              f.write(f"## Turn {turn}\n\n### User{ts}\n\n")      
          else:                                                                                                                                                                    
              f.write(f"### Assistant{ts}\n\n")
          c = entry['content']                                                                                                                                                     
          if len(c) > 15000:                                      
              f.write(c[:15000] + f"\n\n*[... truncated, {len(c)} chars total]*\n\n")
          else:                                                                                                                                                                    
              f.write(c)
          f.write("\n\n---\n\n")                                                                                                                                                   
                                                                  
  print(f"Written: {output_file} ({os.path.getsize(output_file)/1024:.0f} KB, {turn} turns)")                                                                                      
  PYEOF
                                                                                                                                                                                   
  That's it. The script auto-detects the current session and writes the full conversation log.
